\xi^{\prime}_{\ell+1},\dots,\xi^{\prime}_{k-1},s^{\prime}_{k},s^{\prime}_{k+1},\dots,s^{\prime}_{N}

in such a way that (
5.8
) holds.

Lemma 6.1
.

Assume (
4.53
),(
6.1
) and (
5.8
), then there is a constant
C_{1}>0
only depending on the functions
\mathbf{A}
,
\mathbf{E}
,
\mathbf{B}
and
\mathbf{G}
such that

(6.2)

\sum_{i=\ell+1}^{k-1}|\xi_{i}-\xi_{i}^{\prime}|+|s^{\prime}_{k}-\xi_{k}|+\sum_{i=k+1}^{N}|s^{\prime}_{i}|\leq C_{1}|s_{j}|.

Note that the above Lemma holds in both cases of genuinely nonlinear and linearly degenerate
k
-th vector field.

Proof of Lemma
6.1
.

First, we recall (
2.16
) and point out that
\boldsymbol{\beta}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}_{b})=\mathbf{0}_{N}
if and only if
\mathbf{u}_{2}=\mathbf{u}_{2b}
and
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{11}(\mathbf{u})=\boldsymbol{\pi}_{11}(\mathbf{u}_{b})
. In other words,
\boldsymbol{\beta}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}_{b})=\mathbf{0}_{N}
if and only if
\boldsymbol{\pi}(\mathbf{u})=\boldsymbol{\pi}(\mathbf{u}_{b})
, where
\boldsymbol{\pi}
notes the projection onto a suitable
N-\ell
dimensional subspace. Owing to the analysis in
[
3
,
8
,
9
]
we also have that the map at the left hand side of (
5.8
) satisfies the assumptions of
[
14
, Theorem 1]
. Owing to the Lipschitz continuity of the inverse function, by combining (
4.53
), (
6.1
) and (
5.8
) we conclude

\begin{split}\sum_{i=\ell+1}^{k-1}&|\xi_{i}-\xi_{i}^{\prime}|+|s^{\prime}_{k}-\xi_{k}|+\sum_{i=k+1}^{N}|s^{\prime}_{i}|\leq\mathcal{O}(1)|\boldsymbol{\pi}(\mathbf{u}_{b})-\boldsymbol{\pi}\circ{\boldsymbol{\phi}}(\mathbf{u}^{+},\xi_{\ell+1},\dots,\xi_{k-1},\xi_{k})|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:brs0},\eqref{e:lax2}}}{{=}}\mathcal{O}(1)|\boldsymbol{\pi}\circ{\boldsymbol{\phi}}(\cdot,\xi_{\ell+1},\dots,\xi_{k-1},\xi_{k})\circ\mathbf{t}_{j}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{j})-\boldsymbol{\pi}\circ{\boldsymbol{\phi}}(\mathbf{u}^{+},\xi_{\ell+1},\dots,\xi_{k-1},\xi_{k})|\\
&\leq\mathcal{O}(1)|{\boldsymbol{\phi}}(\cdot,\xi_{\ell+1},\dots,\xi_{k-1},\xi_{k})\circ\mathbf{t}_{j}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{j})-{\boldsymbol{\phi}}(\mathbf{u}^{+},\xi_{\ell+1},\dots,\xi_{k-1},\xi_{k})|.\phantom{\int}\end{split}

By the Lipschitz continuity of
{\boldsymbol{\phi}}
with respect to
\mathbf{u}
the above term is controlled by
\mathcal{O}(1)|\mathbf{t}_{j}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{j})-\mathbf{u}^{+}|
and this in turn is controlled by
C_{1}|s_{j}|
for some suitable constant
C_{1}
.
∎

6.2.
Interaction of the boundary with a wave front of the
k
-th family

We now consider the case of a wave front of the
k
-th family hitting the boundary, see Figure
2
(right)
As in the previous paragraph we term
\mathbf{\bar{u}}
and
\mathbf{u}^{+}
the left and the right state of the hitting wave front, respectively, which implies

(6.3)

\mathbf{\bar{u}}=\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}),

for some value
s_{k}
. The estimates we need in the case the hitting wave front belongs to the
k
-th family are much more delicate and precise than (
6.2
), and we obtain them by distinguishing between the genuinely nonlinear case (
2.8
) and the linearly degenerate case (
2.9
).

6.2.1.
Genuinely nonlinear case

We consider the genuinely nonlinear case (
2.8
) and to fix the notation we also assume (
2.11
). The argument in case B) or C) in Hypothesis
5
is entirely
analogous. Note that we have (
4.53
) for some
(\xi_{\ell+1},\dots,\xi_{k})
with
\xi_{k}
satisfying (
4.57
) and (
4.58
), and by combining these inequalities with (
6.3
) we obtain

(6.4)

\xi_{k}\leq\underline{s}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}))\;\text{if}\;\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}))\leq 0,\qquad\xi_{k}=0\;\text{if}\;\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}))>0

and
\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k})<0
. We now consider the values
(\xi^{\prime}_{\ell+1},\dots,\xi^{\prime}_{k-1},s^{\prime}_{k},\dots,s^{\prime}_{N})
satisfying (
5.8
) and state our main interaction estimate.

Lemma 6.2
.

Assume (
2.8
), (
4.53
), (
5.8
), (
6.3
) with
\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k})<0
, and (
6.4
). There is a constant
C_{2}>0
only depending on the functions
\mathbf{A}
,
\mathbf{E}
,
\mathbf{B}
and
\mathbf{G}
and on the value
\mathbf{u}^{\ast}
such that

(6.5)

\sum_{i=\ell+1}^{k-1}|\xi_{i}-\xi_{i}^{\prime}|+|s^{\prime}_{k}-(\xi_{k}+s_{k})|+\sum_{i=k+1}^{N}|s^{\prime}_{i}|\leq C_{2}|s_{k}|\Big([\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k})]^{-}+|\xi_{k}|\Big),

where the symbol
[\cdot]^{-}
denotes the negative part.

As mentioned before, from the technical viewpoint estimate (
6.5
) is one of the key points of our analysis. Its proof is rather long and involved and it is postponed to §
6.3
.

Remark 6.1
.

Note that (
6.2
) is basically “a first order estimate”, because the right hand side is of the same order of the strength
s_{j}
. Conversely, (
6.5
) can be regarded as “a second order estimate” because the right hand side is much smaller than the strength
s_{k}
. The reason why in the case of the waves of the
j
-th family,
j<k
, a “first order estimate” like (
6.2
) suffices is because these waves do not appear after the interaction and hence can be differently weighted, in the same spirit as in
[
1
]
, see (
7.3
).

Remark 6.2
.

Note that the right-hand side of (
6.5
) does not contain any term of the form
|s_{k}||\sum_{i=\ell+1}^{k-1}|\xi_{i}|
. If it did, then our analysis in the next sections would fail. Very loosing speaking, the reason is because the potential
\Upsilon
defined by (
7.4
) should then contain a term like
|s_{k}||\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}|\xi_{i}|
, which in general does not disappear when a
k
-wave hits the domain boundary
x=0
, and hence our proof of the monotonicity of
\Upsilon
would break down.

6.2.2.
Linearly degenerate case

We now focus on the linearly degenerate case (
2.9
). To fix the notation, we also assume case B) in Hypothesis
5
, which is satisfied by the Navier-Stokes and viscous MHD equations in Eulerian coordinates when the fluid velocity vanishes. Note that case C) corresponds to the same equations written in lagrangian coordinates, but in this case no
k
-th wave front can hit the boundary since
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{u})\equiv 0
.

We consider the values
\xi_{\ell(\mathbf{u}_{b})+1},\dots,\xi_{k},s_{k}
and
\xi^{\prime}_{\ell(\mathbf{u}_{b})+1},\dots,\xi^{\prime}_{k-1},s^{\prime}_{k},\dots,s^{\prime}_{N}
satisfying (
6.3
), (
4.59
) and (
5.10
), respectively, and state our main interaction estimate.

Lemma 6.3
.

Assume (
2.9
), (
6.1
), (
4.59
) and (
5.10
). Then

(6.6)

\sum_{\ell(\mathbf{u}_{b})+1}^{k-1}|\xi_{i}-\xi_{i}^{\prime}|+|s^{\prime}_{k}-(\xi_{k}+s_{k})|+\sum_{i=k+1}^{N}|s^{\prime}_{i}|\leq C_{10}|s_{k}|\big(|\xi_{k}|+[\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+})]^{-}\big)

for a suitable constant
C_{10}>0
only depending on
\mathbf{A}
,
\mathbf{E}
,
\mathbf{B}
and
\mathbf{G}
and on the value
\mathbf{u}^{\ast}
.

The proof of Lemma
6.3
relies on the proof of Lemma
6.2
and is given in §
6.5
.

6.3.
Proof of Lemma
6.2
(interaction estimates in the genuinely nonlinear case)

We first establish three preliminary results.

Lemma 6.4
.

Let
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}
be the same function as in (
4.41
), then
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}
is Lipschitz continuous with respect to both the variables
\bf{\widetilde{u}}
and
\xi_{k}
. If
\lambda_{k}(\bf{\widetilde{u}})\geq 0
it is also of class
C^{1,1}
with respect to
\xi
, if

\lambda_{k}(\bf{\widetilde{u}})<0
it is of class
C^{1,1}
on each side of the value
\xi_{k}=\underline{s}(\bf{\widetilde{u}}).

Proof.

All the properties directly follow from the definition (
4.41
), except for the
C^{1,1}
regularity in the case
\lambda_{k}(\bf{\widetilde{u}})\geq 0
: this follows by combining (
4.32
) with (
4.33
).
∎

Lemma 6.5
.

Fix
\mathbf{\widetilde{u}}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
and let
\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},\cdot)
and
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},\cdot)
the curves defined by (
4.12
) and by (
4.41
), respectively. Then

(6.7)

|(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s_{k}),\mathbf{0}_{N-h})-\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s_{k})|\leq\mathcal{O}(1)|s_{k}|[\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s_{k})]^{-}

provided
\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s_{k})
is the same as in (
5.4
).

The proof of Lemma
6.5
is a key step in the proof of (
6.5
) and it is rather technical, but it is based on two basic ingredients: first, the
k
-th vector field is genuinely nonlinear and hence the value
\varsigma_{k}
defined by (
5.4
) can be used to parametrize both

\mathbf{t}_{k}
and
\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{k}
. Second, the proof of (
6.7
) would be easier if
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}
were of class
C^{1,1}
. The function
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}
is unfortunately only Lipschitz continuous, but it is “piecewise of class
C^{1,1}
” and hence one can circumvent the lack of regularity by separately considering different cases (see

Case 1, 2A
and
2B
below). Note however that the lack of regularity accounts for much of the technicality of the proof.

Proof of Lemma
6.5
.

To simplify the exposition, in the proof of the lemma we use the shorthand notation
\varsigma_{k}
for
\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s_{k})
. We also recall the definitions (
4.13
) and (
4.15
) of
\underline{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})
and
\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})
, respectively.
We now separately consider the following cases.

Case 1:
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})\geq 0
. We recall the definition (
4.15
) of
\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})
and conclude that
\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})\leq 0
.
We also recall (
4.41
) and point out that, if
s_{k}\geq\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})
, then the left-hand side of (
6.7
) vanishes and (
6.7
) is trivially satisfied. By definition of
\bar{s}
we have
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}}))=0
and by combining (
4.22
) with the Taylor expansion formula we arrive at

(6.8)

|(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s_{k}),\mathbf{0}_{N-h})-\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s_{k})|\leq\mathcal{O}(1)(\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})-s_{k})^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle s_{k}\leq\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})\leq 0}}{{\leq}}\mathcal{O}(1)(\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})-s_{k})|s_{k}|\quad\text{if $s_{k}\leq\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})$}.

We recall (
4.10
), (
4.15
) and (
5.4
) and point out that for
s_{k}\leq 0

[\varsigma_{k}]^{-}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:baresse},\eqref{e:speedwft}}}{{=}}\int_{s_{k}}^{\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})}\frac{\partial\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{i}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},\tau))}{\partial\tau}d\tau\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:gnl}}}{{\geq}}d(\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})-s_{k}).

By plugging the inequality
(\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})-s_{k})\leq[\varsigma_{k}]^{-}/d
into (
6.8
) we arrive at (
6.7
).

Case 2:
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})<0
, which implies
\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})>0
. Owing to (
4.41
), if
s_{k}\geq\underline{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})
then the left-hand side of (
6.7
) vanishes and (
6.7
) is trivially satisfied. We now distinguish between the cases
s_{k}\leq 0
and
0<s_{k}<\underline{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})
.

Case 2A:
s_{k}\leq 0
. We have

\varsigma_{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:baresse}}}{{=}}-\int_{s_{k}}^{\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})}\frac{\partial\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{i}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},\tau))}{\partial\tau}d\tau\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:gnl}}}{{\leq}}-d(\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})-s_{k})

which yields

(6.9)

\max\{|s_{k}|,\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})>0,s_{k}\leq 0}}{{\leq}}|\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})-s_{k}|\leq\frac{1}{d}[\varsigma_{k}]^{-}.

We set

(6.10)

\mathbf{a}:=\mathbf{i}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}}))\;\text{which implies}\;\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{a})=0,\;\bar{s}(\mathbf{a})=0,\;\mathbf{i}_{k}(\mathbf{a},-\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}}))=\mathbf{\widetilde{u}}

and we refer to Figure
3
for a representation.

Figure 3.
The function
s\mapsto\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{i}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s))
(left) and a representation of the curve
\mathbf{i}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s)
in the phase space (right) in
Case 2A
s
\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})
s_{k}
\mathbf{a}
\mathbf{\widetilde{u}}
\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s_{k})

We also set

(6.11)

\boldsymbol{\varphi}(\tau,s):=\big(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{i}_{k}(\mathbf{a},\tau),s),\mathbf{0}_{N-h}\big)-\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}\big(\mathbf{i}_{k}(\mathbf{a},\tau),s\big)\implies\boldsymbol{\varphi}(-\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}}),s_{k})=(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s_{k}),\mathbf{0}_{N-h})-\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s_{k})

We now recall that
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{a})=0
,
\bar{s}(\mathbf{a})=0
and by using (
6.8
) we get

(6.12)

|\boldsymbol{\varphi}(0,s)|=|\big(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{a},s),\mathbf{0}_{N-h}\big)-\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}\big(\mathbf{a},s\big)|\leq\mathcal{O}(1)|s|^{2},

Also,

(6.13)

\boldsymbol{\varphi}(\tau,0)=\mathbf{0}_{2N-h}\;\text{for every $\tau$}\implies\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{\varphi}}{\partial\tau}(\tau,0)=\mathbf{0}_{2N-h}\;\text{for every $\tau$}.

Since the function
\boldsymbol{\varphi}
is of class
C^{1,1}
on the set
\{(\tau,s):s\leq 0\}
, we have

\begin{split}|&\boldsymbol{\varphi}(-\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}}),s_{k})|=\left|\boldsymbol{\varphi}(0,s_{k})-\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{\varphi}}{\partial\tau}(-\theta\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}}),s_{k})d\theta\right|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:taylor2},\eqref{e:vanish}}}{{\leq}}\mathcal{O}(1)|s_{k}|^{2}+\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})\int_{0}^{1}\left|\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{\varphi}}{\partial\tau}(-\theta\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}}),s_{k})-\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{\varphi}}{\partial\tau}(-\theta\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}}),0)\right|d\theta\leq\mathcal{O}(1)|s_{k}|^{2}+\mathcal{O}(1)\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})|s_{k}|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:pranzo}}}{{\leq}}\frac{\mathcal{O}(1)}{d}|s_{k}|[\varsigma_{k}]^{-}+\frac{\mathcal{O}(1)}{d}|s_{k}|[\varsigma_{k}]^{-}\end{split}

and owing to (
6.11
) this yields (
6.7
).

Case 2B:

0<s_{k}<\underline{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})
. Owing to (
4.41
) we have

(6.14)

(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s_{k}),\mathbf{0}_{N-h})-\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s_{k})=(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s_{k}),\mathbf{0}_{N-h})-\mathbf{b}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s_{k})

We recall that
\sigma_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s)
denotes the speed of the shock joining
(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}}
(on the right) with
\mathbf{h}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s)
(on the left) and that the function
\mathbf{h}_{k}
is defined by (
4.11
). Next, we consider the function

\boldsymbol{\ell}(\tau,s):=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\sigma_{k}\big(\mathbf{i}_{k}(\mathbf{a},\tau),s\big)\\
s\\
\end{array}\right),

where
\mathbf{a}
is the same as in (
6.10
).
Note that

(6.15)

\boldsymbol{\ell}(-\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}}),s):=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\sigma_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s)\\
s\\
\end{array}\right),\qquad\qquad\mathbf{D}\boldsymbol{\ell}(0,0)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\displaystyle{\frac{\partial\sigma_{k}}{\partial\tau}}&\displaystyle{\frac{\partial\sigma}{\partial s}}\\
0&1\\
\end{array}\right).

Since
\sigma_{k}\big(\mathbf{i}_{k}(\mathbf{a},\tau),0\big)=\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{i}_{k}(\mathbf{a},\tau))
, then

(6.16)

\left.\frac{\partial\sigma_{k}}{\partial\tau}(\mathbf{i}_{k}(\mathbf{a},\tau),0)\right|_{\tau=0}=\nabla\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{a})\cdot\mathbf{r}_{k}(\mathbf{a})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:gnl}}}{{\geq}}d>0

and hence the function
\boldsymbol{\ell}
is locally invertible in a neighborhood of the origin
(0,0)
. Next, we set

(6.17)

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\tau,s):=\big(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{i}_{k}(\mathbf{a},\tau),s),\mathbf{0}_{N-h}\big)-\mathbf{b}_{k}\big(\mathbf{i}_{k}(\mathbf{a},\tau),s\big)

and point out that

(6.18)

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(-\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}}),s)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:a}}}{{=}}\big(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s),\mathbf{0}_{N-h}\big)-\mathbf{b}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s),

which is the right-hand side of (
6.14
). Also, we set

(6.19)

\underline{\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\sigma,s):=\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varphi}}\circ\boldsymbol{\ell}^{-1}(\sigma,s)

and point out that

(6.20)

\boldsymbol{\ell}^{-1}(\sigma_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s),s)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:li}}}{{=}}(-\bar{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}}),s)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:li2}}}{{\implies}}\underline{\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\sigma_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s),s)=\big(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s),\mathbf{0}_{N-h}\big)-\mathbf{b}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s).

Assume for the time being that we have established the equalities

(6.21)

\underline{\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(0,s)=\mathbf{0}_{2N-h}\;\text{for every $s\geq 0$},\quad\mathbf{\underline{g}}(\sigma,0)=\mathbf{0}_{2N-h}\;\text{for every $\sigma$}.

The function
\underline{\boldsymbol{\varphi}}
is of class
C^{2}
since both
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varphi}}
and
\boldsymbol{\ell}
are of class
C^{2}
. We can then argue as in the proof of
[
10
, Lemma 2.5]
and conclude that

|\underline{\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\sigma,s)|\leq\mathcal{O}(1)|\sigma||s|,\;\text{for every $s\geq 0$}.

Owing to (
6.20
) and to the inequality
s_{k}\geq 0
, this implies that the modulus of the right-hand side of (
6.14
) is bounded by
\mathcal{O}(1)|\sigma_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s_{k})||s_{k}|
. By recalling that
\sigma_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s_{k})<0
because
s_{k}<\underline{s}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})
and by using (
5.4
) this concludes the proof of (
6.7
). We are thus left to establish (
6.21
). To establish the first equality in (
6.21
), we recall that
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{a})=0
, which in turn yields

(6.22)

\sigma_{k}(\mathbf{i}_{k}(\mathbf{a},0),s)=\sigma_{k}(\mathbf{a},s)\geq 0\;\text{for every $s\geq 0$}.

Owing to (
6.16
), the partial derivative
\partial\sigma_{k}(\mathbf{i}_{k}(\mathbf{a},\tau),s)/\partial\tau
is strictly positive in a neighborhood of the origin. Owing to (
6.22
), this implies that if
\sigma_{k}(\mathbf{i}_{k}(\mathbf{a},\tau),s)=0
for some
s\geq 0
, then
\tau\leq 0
, which in turn implies
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{i}_{k}(\mathbf{a},\tau))\leq 0
and
s=\underline{s}(\mathbf{i}_{k}(\mathbf{a},\tau))
. We now recall (
6.17
) and (
6.19
) and infer that

\begin{split}\underline{\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(0,s)=\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\tau,\underline{s}(\mathbf{i}_{k}(\mathbf{a},\tau)))&=\big(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{i}_{k}(\mathbf{a},\tau),\underline{s}(\mathbf{i}_{k}(\mathbf{a},\tau)),\mathbf{0}_{N-h}\big)-\mathbf{b}_{k}(\mathbf{i}_{k}(\mathbf{a},\tau),\underline{s}(\mathbf{i}_{k}(\mathbf{a},\tau))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:pera}}}{{=}}\mathbf{0}_{2N-h}\\
&\quad\text{for every $s\geq 0$},\end{split}

which establishes the first equality in (
6.21
). To establish the second one we point out that

\begin{split}\underline{\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\sigma,0)&=\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varphi}}(\tau,0)=\big(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{i}_{k}(\mathbf{a},\tau),0),\mathbf{0}_{N-h}\big)-\mathbf{b}_{k}(\mathbf{i}_{k}(\mathbf{a},\tau),0)=\big(\mathbf{i}_{k}(\mathbf{a},\tau),\mathbf{0}_{N-h}\big)-\big(\mathbf{i}_{k}(\mathbf{a},\tau),\mathbf{0}_{N-h}\big)\\
&=\mathbf{0}_{2N-h}\quad\text{for $\tau$ such that
$\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{i}_{k}(\mathbf{a},\tau))=\sigma$.}\end{split}

This concludes the proof of (
6.21
) and hence of (
6.7
).
∎

Lemma 6.6
.

Under the same assumptions as in the statement of Lemma
6.2
we have

(6.23)

|\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}),\xi_{k})-\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}+\xi_{k})|\leq\mathcal{O}(1)|s_{k}|([\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k})]^{-}+|\xi_{k}|)

provided the curve
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}
is the same as in (
4.41
).

Note that by combining (
6.23
) with (
4.42
) we get in particular

(6.24)

|\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}),\xi_{k})-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}+\xi_{k})|\leq\mathcal{O}(1)|s_{k}|([\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k})]^{-}+|\xi_{k}|)

Proof of Lemma
6.6
.

To simplify the exposition, in the proof of the lemma we use the shorthand notation
\varsigma_{k}
for
\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k})
.
We separately consider the following cases.

Case 1:

\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+})\geq 0
. By combining the definition (
5.4
) of
\varsigma_{k}
with the inequality
\varsigma_{k}<0
we conclude that
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}))<0
and hence that
s_{k}<\bar{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+})\leq 0
.
Owing to (
6.4
) this implies
\xi_{k}\leq\underline{s}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k})).
We set

(6.25)

\mathbf{q}(s,\xi):=\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s),\xi)-\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s+\xi)

and we consider the function
\mathbf{q}
defined on the set

(6.26)

E:=\{(s,\xi):\;s\leq\bar{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+}),\;\xi\leq\underline{s}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s))\},

see Figure
4
.

Figure 4.
The set
E
in (
6.26
)
s
\bar{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+})
\xi
E
\underline{s}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s))

Note that
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},\cdot)
is of class
C^{1,1}
since
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+})\geq 0
by assumption, and hence
\mathbf{q}
is of class
C^{1,1}
with respect to the variable
\xi
on
E
owing to Lemma
6.4
.
We now evaluate
\mathbf{q}(\bar{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+}),\xi)
.
By definition (
4.15
) of
\bar{s}
,
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},\bar{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+})))=0
and this yields

(6.27)

\underline{s}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},\bar{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+})))=0=\bar{s}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},\bar{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+})))

If
\xi\leq\underline{s}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},\bar{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+})))=0
then by the third definition at the right hand-side of (
4.41
) and because of (
6.27
) we have
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},\bar{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+})),\xi)=\mathbf{b}_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},\bar{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+})),\xi)
. Also, if
\xi\leq 0
then
\bar{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+})+\xi\leq\bar{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+})
and hence (
4.41
) yields
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},\bar{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+})+\xi)=\mathbf{b}_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},\bar{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+})),\xi)
.
We conclude that

(6.28)

\mathbf{q}(\bar{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+}),\xi)=\mathbf{0}_{2N-h}\quad\text{for every
$\xi\leq\underline{s}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},\bar{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+}))=0$},

and this yields

(6.29)

\frac{\partial\mathbf{q}}{\partial\xi}(\bar{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+}),\xi)=\mathbf{0}_{2N-h}\quad\text{for every $\xi\leq\underline{s}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},\bar{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+}))=0$.}

If
(s_{k},\xi_{k})\in E
and
\xi_{k}<0
we then have

(6.30)

\begin{split}|\mathbf{q}(s_{k},\xi_{k})|&=\left|\mathbf{q}(s_{k},0)+\xi_{k}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\partial\mathbf{q}}{\partial\xi}(s_{k},\theta\xi_{k})d\theta\right|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:pi},\eqref{e:kiwi}}}{{\leq}}|\underbrace{\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}),0)}_{\displaystyle{=(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}),\mathbf{0}_{N-h})}}-\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k})|+\left|\xi_{k}\int_{0}^{1}\left(\frac{\partial\mathbf{q}}{\partial\xi}(s_{k},\theta\xi_{k})-\frac{\partial\mathbf{q}}{\partial\xi}(\bar{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+}),\theta\xi_{k})\right)d\theta\right|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:me1}}}{{\leq}}\mathcal{O}(1)|s_{k}|[\varsigma_{k}]^{-}+\mathcal{O}(1)|\xi_{k}||s_{k}-\bar{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+})|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle s_{k}\leq\bar{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+})\leq 0}}{{\leq}}\mathcal{O}(1)|s_{k}|([\varsigma_{k}]^{-}+|\xi_{k}|),\end{split}

that is (
6.24
). For
(s_{k},\xi_{k})\in E
,
\xi_{k}\geq 0
we have by
C^{1,1}
regularity

(6.31)

\begin{split}|\mathbf{q}(s_{k},\xi_{k})|&=\left|\mathbf{q}(s_{k},0)+\xi_{k}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\partial\mathbf{q}}{\partial\xi}(s_{k},\theta\xi_{k})d\theta\right|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:pi}}}{{\leq}}|(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}),\mathbf{0}_{N-h})-\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k})|+\left|\xi_{k}\int_{0}^{1}\!\!\!\left(\frac{\partial\mathbf{q}}{\partial\xi}(s_{k},\theta\xi_{k})-\frac{\partial\mathbf{q}}{\partial\xi}(s_{k},0)\right)d\theta+\xi_{k}\frac{\partial\mathbf{q}}{\partial\xi}(s_{k},0)\right|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:me1}}}{{\leq}}\mathcal{O}(1)|s_{k}|[\varsigma_{k}]^{-}+\mathcal{O}(1)|\xi_{k}|^{2}+|\xi_{k}|\left|\frac{\partial\mathbf{q}}{\partial\xi}(s_{k},0)\right|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:kiwi}}}{{\leq}}\mathcal{O}(1)|s_{k}|[\varsigma_{k}]^{-}+\mathcal{O}(1)|\xi_{k}|^{2}+|\xi_{k}|\left|\frac{\partial\mathbf{q}}{\partial\xi}(s_{k},0)-\frac{\partial\mathbf{q}}{\partial\xi}(\bar{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+}),0)\right|\\
&\leq\mathcal{O}(1)(|s_{k}|[\varsigma_{k}]^{-}+|\xi_{k}|^{2}+|\xi_{k}||s_{k}-\bar{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+})|)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle s_{k}\leq\bar{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+})\leq 0}}{{\leq}}\mathcal{O}(1)(|s_{k}|[\varsigma_{k}]^{-}+|\xi_{k}|^{2}+|\xi_{k}||s_{k}|)\end{split}

To conclude, we point out that we are considering the case

\begin{split}0\leq\xi_{k}\leq\underline{s}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}))\implies|\xi_{k}|&\leq|\underline{s}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}))|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:autunno}}}{{=}}|\underline{s}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}))-\underline{s}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},\bar{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+}))|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:lipunders}}}{{\leq}}\mathcal{O}(1)|s_{k}-\bar{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+})|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle s_{k}<\bar{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+})\leq 0}}{{\leq}}\mathcal{O}(1)|s_{k}|\end{split}

and by plugging the above inequality into (
6.31
) we arrive at (
6.23
).

Case 2:

\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+})<0
and
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}))>0
. Owing to (
6.4
) we have
\xi_{k}=0
, which yields

|\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}),\xi_{k})-\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}+\xi_{k})|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\xi_{k}=0}}{{=}}|(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}),\mathbf{0}_{N-h})-\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k})|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:me1}}}{{\leq}}\mathcal{O}(1)|s_{k}|[\varsigma_{k}]^{-},

which in turn implies (
6.23
).

Case 3:

\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+})<0
and
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}))\leq 0
, which yields
s_{k}\leq\underline{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+})
and hence, owing to (
4.41
),

(6.32)

\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k})=\mathbf{b}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}).

Owing to (
6.4
) we have

\xi_{k}\leq\underline{s}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}))
and hence, owing to (
4.41
),

(6.33)

\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}),\xi_{k})=\mathbf{b}_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}),\xi_{k}).

We now separately consider the following two sub-cases.

Case 3A:

s_{k}+\xi_{k}\leq\underline{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+}).
Owing to (
4.41
) and recalling (
6.33
) this implies
\mathbf{q}(s_{k},\xi_{k})=\mathbf{\widetilde{q}}(s_{k},\xi_{k})
, where
\mathbf{q}
is the same as in (
6.25
) and

(6.34)

\mathbf{\widetilde{q}}(s,\xi):=\mathbf{b}_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s),\xi)-\mathbf{b}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s+\xi)

Note that
\mathbf{\widetilde{q}}
is a function of class
C^{2}
satisfying
\mathbf{\widetilde{q}}(0,\xi)=0
for every
\xi
, and hence
\frac{\partial\mathbf{\widetilde{q}}}{\partial\xi}(0,\xi)=0
for every
\xi
. This yields

(6.35)

\begin{split}|\mathbf{\widetilde{q}}(s_{k},\xi_{k})|&=\left|\mathbf{\widetilde{q}}(s_{k},0)+\xi_{k}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\partial\mathbf{\widetilde{q}}}{\partial\xi}(s_{k},\theta\xi_{k})d\theta\right|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:tildeq}}}{{=}}|(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}),\mathbf{0}_{N-h})-\mathbf{b}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k})|+\left|\xi_{k}\int_{0}^{1}\left(\frac{\partial\mathbf{\widetilde{q}}}{\partial\xi}(s_{k},\theta\xi_{k})-\frac{\partial\mathbf{\widetilde{q}}}{\partial\xi}(0,\theta\xi_{k})\right)d\theta\right|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:me1},\eqref{e:fabioandrea}}}{{\leq}}\mathcal{O}(1)|s|[\varsigma_{k}]^{-}+\mathcal{O}(1)|\xi_{k}||s_{k}|,\end{split}

and this implies (
6.23
).

Case 3B:

s_{k}+\xi_{k}>\underline{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+}).
Owing to (
4.41
), this implies
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}+\xi_{k})=(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}+\xi_{k}),\mathbf{0}_{N-h})
and yields

(6.36)

\begin{split}|\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}&(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}),\xi_{k})-\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}+\xi_{k})|=|\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}),\xi_{k})-(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}+\xi_{k}),\mathbf{0}_{N-h})|\\
&\leq|\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}),\xi_{k})-\big(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}),\xi_{k}),\mathbf{0}_{N-h}\big)|+|\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}),\xi_{k})-\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}+\xi_{k})|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:me1}}}{{\leq}}\mathcal{O}(1)[\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}),\xi_{k})]^{-}|\xi_{k}|+|\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}),\xi_{k})-\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}+\xi_{k})|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:speedwft}}}{{=}}\mathcal{O}(1)[\sigma_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}),\xi_{k})]^{-}|\xi_{k}|+|\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}),\xi_{k})-\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}+\xi_{k})|.\end{split}

To establish the last equality, we have used the fact that
\xi_{k}\geq 0
since
\xi_{k}\geq\underline{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+})-s_{k}
(because we are dealing with
Step 3B
) and
\underline{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+})-s_{k}\geq 0
(because
\varsigma_{k}\leq 0
).

To control the right-hand side of (
6.36
), we first point out that, owing to genuine nonlinearity (
2.8
), the map
\xi\mapsto[\sigma_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}),\xi)]^{-}
is monotone decreasing. Since
\xi_{k}\geq\underline{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+})-s_{k}
then

(6.37)

[\sigma_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}),\xi_{k})]^{-}\leq[\sigma_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}),\underline{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+})-s_{k})]^{-}.

We now set
q(s):=[\sigma_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s),\underline{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+})-s)]^{-}
and we point out that
q
is a Lipschitz continuous function which, owing
to the definition (
4.13
) of
\underline{s}
, satisfies

q(0)=0
. This implies that
|q(s)|\leq\mathcal{O}(1)|s|
and by plugging this inequality into (
6.37
) we arrive at

(6.38)

[\sigma_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}),\xi_{k})]^{-}\leq\mathcal{O}(1)|s_{k}|.

To control the second term at the right-hand side of (
6.36
) we set
\mathbf{\underline{q}}(s,\xi):=\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s),\xi)-\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s+\xi)
and point out that
\mathbf{\underline{q}}
is a
C^{2}
function satisfying
\mathbf{\underline{q}}(s,0)=\mathbf{0}_{N}
for every
s
and
\mathbf{\underline{q}}(0,\xi)=\mathbf{0}_{N}
for every
\xi
. Owing to
[
10
, Lemma 2.5]
this implies
|\mathbf{\underline{q}}(s,\xi)|\leq\mathcal{O}(1)|s||\xi|
and by plugging this inequality into (
6.36
) and recalling (
6.38
) we arrive at (
6.23
).
∎

6.3.1.
Proof of Lemma
6.2

By arguing as in the proof of Lemma
6.1
we arrive at

\begin{split}\sum_{i=\ell+1}^{k-1}&|\xi_{i}-\xi_{i}^{\prime}|+|s^{\prime}_{k}-(\xi_{k}+s_{k})|+\sum_{i=k+1}^{N}|s^{\prime}_{i}|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:brs0},\eqref{e:lax}}}{{\leq}}|{\boldsymbol{\phi}}(\cdot,\xi_{\ell+1},\dots,\xi_{k-1},\xi_{k})\circ\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k})-{\boldsymbol{\phi}}(\mathbf{u}^{+},\xi_{\ell+1},\dots,\xi_{k-1},\xi_{k}+s_{k})|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:bl10}}}{{\leq}}|{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{s}(\cdot,\xi_{\ell+1},\dots,\xi_{k-1})\circ\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{k}(\cdot,\xi_{k})\circ\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k})-{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{s}(\cdot,\xi_{\ell+1},\dots,\xi_{k-1})\circ\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},\xi_{k}+s_{k})|\\
&\quad+|{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{p}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k}),\xi_{\ell+1},\dots,\xi_{k-1},\xi_{k})-{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{p}(\mathbf{u}^{+},\xi_{\ell+1},\dots,\xi_{k-1},\xi_{k}+s_{k})|:=\mathcal{E}.\phantom{\int}\end{split}

By using the Lipschitz continuity of both
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{s}
and
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{p}
we then get

\begin{split}\mathcal{E}&\leq\mathcal{O}(1)|\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{k}(\cdot,\xi_{k})\circ\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k})-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},\xi_{k}+s_{k})|+|\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}(\cdot,\xi_{k})\circ\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k})-\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},\xi_{k}+s_{k})|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:anguria2},\eqref{e:anguria}}}{{\leq}}\mathcal{O}(1)|s_{k}|([\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k})]^{-}+|\xi_{k}|),\end{split}

that is (
6.5
).

6.4.
Further boundary interaction estimates in the genuinely nonlinear case

In this paragraph we collect some further results (Lemmas
6.7
,
6.8
and
6.9
)concerning the interaction between a wave front of the
k
-th family and the boundary. We will use them in the following sections.

Lemma 6.7
.

Under the same assumptions as in Lemma
6.2
, there is a sufficiently small constant
\nu>0
such that, if
[\sigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k})]^{-}+|\xi_{k}|\leq\nu
, then the following holds. Assume
s_{k}>0

and let
\mathbf{\hat{u}}
be the same as in (
5.9
);
then
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})<0
.

Remark 6.3
.

The meaning of Lemma
6.7
is the following. Assume that a
k
-th family wave front hits the boundary, and that the wave front is a shock, which implies
s_{k}>0
. Then, if there is a
k
-th family wave front entering the domain after the interaction, this wave front must be a shock. Indeed,
\mathbf{\hat{u}}
is the right state of the wave front and the condition
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})<0
dictates that
\mathbf{\hat{u}}
cannot be the right state of a
k
-th family rarefaction wave with positive speed.

The reason why we need the above Lemma
6.7
(or, more precisely, its consequence discussed in Remark
6.3
) is because we want to have that every wave front can be uniquely continued forward in time, unless it gets canceled by the interaction with a front of the same family with opposite side or it is absorbed by the boundary. If a shock could hit the boundary and be reflected as a rarefaction, then by the construction in §
5.3
this rarefaction could be partitioned and hence the original wave front could not be uniquely continued forward in time.

Proof of Lemma
6.7
.

We proceed according to the following steps.

Step 1:
we show that

(6.39)

[\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+})]^{-}\geq\frac{d}{2}s_{k},

provided
d
is the same constant as in (
2.8
). To this end, we first of all point out that the Lax entropy condition combined with the inequality
\sigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k})<0
yields
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+})<0
and also
s_{k}<\underline{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+})
. Recalling that
s_{k}>0
by assumption, this implies that to establish (
6.39
) it suffices to show that

(6.40)

[\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+})]^{-}\geq\frac{d}{2}\underline{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+}).

Owing to (
2.8
) we have
\partial\sigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s)/ds\geq d/2
provided
s
is small enough. This implies

0=\sigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},\underline{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+}))=\sigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},0)+\int_{0}^{\underline{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+})}\frac{\partial\sigma_{k}}{\partial s}ds=\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+})+\int_{0}^{\underline{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+})}\frac{\partial\sigma_{k}}{\partial s}ds\geq\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+})+\frac{d}{2}\underline{s}(\mathbf{u}^{+})

and this yields (
6.40
) and henceforth (
6.39
).

Step 2:
we conclude the proof. We point out that

\begin{split}\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})&=\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+})+\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})-\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:bdsulk}}}{{\leq}}-\frac{d}{2}s_{k}+\mathcal{O}(1)|\mathbf{\hat{u}}-\mathbf{u}^{+}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:hatu}}}{{\leq}}-\frac{d}{2}s_{k}+\mathcal{O}(1)\sum_{i=k+1}^{N}|s^{\prime}_{i}|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:me}}}{{\leq}}s_{k}\left(-\frac{d}{2}+\mathcal{O}(1)([\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k})]^{-}+|\xi_{k}|)\right)<0\end{split}

provided
[\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k})]^{-}+|\xi_{k}|
is sufficiently small. By recalling (
5.4
) and the inequality
s_{k}>0
we get
\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k})=\sigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k})
and this concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 6.8
.

Under the same assumptions as in the statement of Lemma
6.2
, there is a sufficiently small constant
\nu>0
such that, if
[\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k})]^{-}+|\xi_{k}|\leq\nu
, then the following holds. Assume that
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+})<0
,
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})>0
, where
\mathbf{\hat{u}}
is the same as in (
5.9
), and
s^{\prime}_{k}<0
; then

(6.41)

\min\{|s^{\prime}_{k}|,|\bar{s}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})|\}\leq C_{8}|s_{k}|(|\xi_{k}|+[\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k})]^{-})

for a suitable constant
C_{8}>0
only depending on the functions
\mathbf{A}
,
\mathbf{E}
,
\mathbf{B}
and
\mathbf{G}
and on the value
\mathbf{u}^{\ast}
.

The meaning of the above lemma is the following. Assume that there is a
k
-th family wave front hitting the boundary and that after the interaction there is a
k
-th family rarefaction wave front enterning the domain. Owing to Lemma
6.7
, this dictates that the hitting wave is a rarefaction front and owing to (
5.5
) yields
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+})<0
. Lemma
6.8
dictates that the left-hand side of (
6.41
), which is the size of the rarefaction wave front entering the domain after the interaction, is actually fairly small as it is controlled by the right hand side of (
6.41
).

Proof.

It suffices to show that
|\bar{s}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})|\leq C_{8}|s_{k}|(|\xi_{k}|+[\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k})]^{-})
. We have

(6.42)

\begin{split}|\bar{s}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})|&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:gnl}}}{{\leq}}\frac{2}{d}\big(\lambda_{k}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})-\underbrace{\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\hat{\mathbf{u}},\bar{s}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})))}_{=0\;\text{by~\eqref{e:baresse}}}\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+})<0}}{{\leq}}\frac{2}{d}\big(\lambda_{k}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})-\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+})\big)\leq\mathcal{O}(1)|\hat{\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{u}^{+}|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:hatu}}}{{\leq}}\mathcal{O}(1)\sum_{i=k+1}^{N}|s^{\prime}_{i}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:me}}}{{\leq}}C_{8}|s_{k}|(|\xi_{k}|+[\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{k})]^{-}),\end{split}

and this yields (
6.41
).
∎

We now consider an interaction where the wave hitting the boundary belongs to the
j
-th family,
j<k
, and combine (
6.1
) with (
4.57
) to get

(6.43)

\xi_{k}\leq\underline{s}(\mathbf{t}_{j}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{j}))\;\text{if}\;\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{j}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{j}))\leq 0,\qquad\xi_{k}=0\;\text{if}\;\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{j}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{j}))>0.

Lemma 6.9
.

Under the same assumptions as in the statement of Lemma
6.1
, assume (
6.43
), let
\mathbf{\hat{u}}
be as in (
5.9
) and assume that

(6.44)

\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})\geq 0,\quad s^{\prime}_{k}\leq\bar{s}(\mathbf{\hat{u}}).

Then

(6.45)

|\bar{s}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})|\leq C_{12}|s_{j}|.

for a suitable constant
C_{12}>0
only depending on the functions
\mathbf{A}
,
\mathbf{E}
,
\mathbf{B}
and
\mathbf{G}
and on the value
\mathbf{u}^{\ast}
.

The heuristic meaning of the above lemma is the following. Owing to the analysis of case iii) in §
4.6.1
, assumption (
6.44
) implies that there is a rarefaction wave front of the
k
-th family entering the domain with size
\bar{s}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})
. Estimate (
6.45
) dictates that, no matter how large
\xi_{k}
is, the size of this rarefaction wave front is controlled by the strength of the hitting wave.

Proof of Lemma
6.9
.

We separately consider the following cases.

Case 1:
if
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{j}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{j}))\leq 0
then we can follow the same computations as in (
6.42
) and conclude that

\begin{split}|\bar{s}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})|&\leq\mathcal{O}(1)|\mathbf{t}_{j}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{j})-\mathbf{\hat{u}}|\leq\mathcal{O}(1)|\mathbf{t}_{j}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{j})-\mathbf{u}^{+}|+\mathcal{O}(1)|\mathbf{u}^{+}-\mathbf{\hat{u}}|\\
&\leq\mathcal{O}(1)|s_{j}|+\mathcal{O}(1)|\sum_{j=k+1}^{N}|s^{\prime}_{j}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:nc}}}{{\leq}}\mathcal{O}(1)|s_{j}|.\end{split}

Case 2:
if
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{j}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{j}))>0
then
\xi_{k}=0
by (
6.43
), which owing to (
6.2
) yields

(6.46)

|s^{\prime}_{k}|\leq C_{1}|s_{j}|.

We recall that
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})\geq 0
by (
6.44
),
and by (
4.15
) this implies
\bar{s}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})\leq 0
, which combined with the second inequality in (
6.44
) yields

|\bar{s}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})|\leq|s^{\prime}_{k}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:galaverna2}}}{{\leq}}C_{1}|s_{j}|,

and this eventually concludes the proof of (
6.45
).
∎

6.5.
Proof of Lemma
6.3
(interaction estimate in the linearly degenerate case)

Lemma
6.3
can be established by combining the proof of Lemma
6.2
in §
6.3.1
with Lemma
6.10
below, which can be viewed as the linearly degenerate equivalent of Lemma
6.6
. We recall (
4.35
), the construction in §
4.4
and in particular that
\mathbf{b}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s)
is the solution of the Cauchy problem (
4.36
) evaluated at
\tau=s
. Next, we set

(6.47)

\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s),0)&\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})\geq 0\\
\mathbf{b}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s)&\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})<0,\\
\end{array}\right.

Lemma 6.10
.

Assume that the
k
-th characteristic field is linearly degenerate (
2.9
) and let
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}
be the same as in (
6.47
);
then

(6.48)

|\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}\big(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s),\xi\big)-\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s+\xi)|\leq\mathcal{O}(1)|s|\big(|\xi|+[\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})]^{-}).

Proof.

We proceed according to the following steps.

Step 1:
we assume
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})\geq 0
. This implies that
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s))\geq 0
because the
k
-th characteristic field is linearly degenerate
and by (
6.47
) the left-hand side of (
6.48
) boils down to

|(\mathbf{t}_{k}\big(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s),\xi\big),0)-(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s+\xi),0)|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:lax:lindeg}}}{{=}}0,

which implies that (
6.48
) is trivially satisfied.

Step 2:
we assume
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})<0
. Owing to (
4.35
) in this case the left-hand side of (
6.48
) boils down to

|\mathbf{b}_{k}\big(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s),\xi\big)-\mathbf{b}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s+\xi)|
.
Note that by classical properties of the Cauchy problem the function
\mathbf{b}_{k}
is of class
C^{2}
with respect to both its arguments. We conclude that the function

\mathbf{h}(s,\xi):=\mathbf{b}_{k}\big(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s),\xi\big)-\mathbf{b}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s+\xi)

is of class
C^{2}
. Also, it satisfies the equalities

(6.49)

\mathbf{h}(0,\xi)=\mathbf{0}_{N+1}\;\text{for every $\xi$}\implies\frac{\partial\mathbf{h}}{\partial\xi}(0,\xi)=\mathbf{0}_{N+1}\;\text{for every $\xi$},

which in turn yield

(6.50)

\begin{split}|\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}&\big(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s),\xi\big)-\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s+\xi)|=|\mathbf{h}(s,\xi)|=\left|\mathbf{h}(s,0)+\xi\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\partial\mathbf{h}}{\partial\xi}(s,\theta\xi)d\theta\right|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:mfacca}}}{{\leq}}|\mathbf{h}(s,0)|+\left|\xi\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\partial\mathbf{h}}{\partial\xi}(s,\theta\xi)-\frac{\partial\mathbf{h}}{\partial\xi}(0,\theta\xi)d\theta\right|\leq|\mathbf{h}(s,0)|+\mathcal{O}(1)|s||\xi|.\end{split}

Assume that we have established the inequality

(6.51)

|\mathbf{h}(s,0)|=|(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s),0)-\mathbf{b}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s)|\leq\mathcal{O}(1)|s||\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})|

then by plugging (
6.51
) into (
6.50
) and recalling that we are handling the case
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})<0
we establish (
6.48
).

Step 3:
we establish (
6.51
). First, we recall (
4.36
) and point out that

(6.52)

\begin{split}\frac{d|\mathbf{u}(\tau)-\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},\tau)|}{d\tau}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:lax:lindeg},\eqref{e:cplindeg}}}{{\leq}}|\mathbf{r}_{c}(\mathbf{u},z_{00},0)-\mathbf{r}_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},\cdot))|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:errecl}}}{{\leq}}\mathcal{O}(1)|\mathbf{u}-\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},\cdot)|+\mathcal{O}(1)|z_{00}|+\mathcal{O}(1)|\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},\cdot))|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:lindeg}}}{{=}}\mathcal{O}(1)|\mathbf{u}-\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},\cdot)|+\mathcal{O}(1)|z_{00}|+\mathcal{O}(1)|\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})|\end{split}

Next,

(6.53)

\begin{split}\frac{d|z_{00}|(\tau)|}{d\tau}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:cplindeg}}}{{\leq}}|\theta_{00}(\mathbf{u},z_{00},0)|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:errecl}}}{{\leq}}\mathcal{O}(1)|z_{00}|+\mathcal{O}(1)|\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{u})|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:lindeg}}}{{\leq}}\mathcal{O}(1)|z_{00}|+\mathcal{O}(1)|\mathbf{u}-\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},\cdot)|+\mathcal{O}(1)|\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})|\end{split}

and by combining (
6.52
) and (
6.53
) with the Comparison Theorem for solutions of ODEs we arrive at

|\mathbf{u}(s)-\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s)|+|z_{00}(s)|\leq\mathcal{O}(1)|\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})|[\exp(\mathcal{O}(1)|s|)-1]\leq\mathcal{O}(1)|\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})||s|,

that is (
6.51
).
∎

7.
Total variation bounds

In this section we show a uniform in time and
\varepsilon
bound on the space total variation of the
\varepsilon
-wave front-tracking approximation constructed in §
5
.
As it is by now fairly customary in the wave front-tracking business, the proof of the total variation bounds relies on the introduction of a Glimm-type functional. Note however that while our functional
\Upsilon
contains some terms that have been already introduced in
[
10
, p.136-7]
, we also introduce completely new terms accounting for the interaction of wave fronts of the
k
-th (boundary characteristic) family with the boundary.
As a matter of fact, the explicit form of
\Upsilon
is a consequence of the interaction estimates established in the previous section and is one of the main novelties of the present paper.

Our functional is well-defined and piecewise constant in
t
as long as the total number of wave fronts is finite. Lemma
8.1
in the next section states that this is actually the case for every
t
. In this section we show that, as long as it well-defined,
\Upsilon
is a monotone non-increasing function with respect to time. More precisely, we proceed as follows: we fix a time
t
at which either two wave fronts cross each other or a wave front hits the boundary, or the boundary datum
\mathbf{u}_{b}^{\varepsilon}
has a jump discontinuity
16
16

16

By changing of an arbitrary small amount the speed of the wave fronts, we can assume (i) that at a given time at most two wave fronts can cross each other (no triple or multiple interactions); (ii) that at a given time at most one of the following events can occur: two wave fronts cross each other, a wave front hits the boundary and the boundary datum has a discontinuity.
See also Remark 7.1 at page 132 and property (FT1) at page 142 in
[
10
]
. We assume that

(7.1)

\Upsilon(t^{-}):=\lim_{\tau\to t^{-}}\Upsilon(\tau)\leq\delta

and we show that if the constant
\delta>0
is sufficiently small then
\Upsilon(t^{+}):=\lim_{\tau\to t^{+}}\Upsilon(\tau)\leq\Upsilon(t^{-})
. We actually establish a more precise estimate and we exactly quantify the difference
\Upsilon(t^{+})-\Upsilon(t^{-})
, as we need this more precise information in the following sections. Note that showing that
\Upsilon
is monotone non-increasing immediately yields a uniform control on the total variation owing to the estimate

(7.2)

\mathrm{TotVar}\ \mathbf{u}_{\varepsilon}(t,\cdot)\leq\mathcal{O}(1)\Upsilon(t)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{$\Upsilon$ monotone}}}{{\leq}}\mathcal{O}(1)\Upsilon(0)\leq C_{14}\delta^{\ast}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:delta1}}}{{\leq}}\delta,

where
\delta^{\ast}
is the same as in (
1.7
) and
C_{14}>0
is a suitable constant only depending on
\mathbf{g}
,
\mathbf{f}
,
\mathbf{D}
and on the change of variables
\mathbf{u}
, and on the value
\mathbf{u}^{\ast}
in (
2.7
). The first and last inequalities in the above estimate directly follow from the explicit form of
\Upsilon
given below, see (
7.4
).

The exposition in this section is organized as follows. In §
7.1
we define the functional
\Upsilon
. In
7.2
we consider the case of a wave front of the family
j
,
j<k
, that hits the boundary, in §
7.3
we deal with a wave front of the
k
-th family hitting the boundary and in §
7.4
with a jump discontinuity of
\mathbf{u}_{b}^{\varepsilon}
. In §
7.5
we discuss the interaction of two wave fronts inside the domain.
In this section we use the notation
\Delta\Upsilon(t):=\Upsilon(t^{+})-\Upsilon(t^{-})
.

Remark 7.1
.

To ease the exposition, in the following we carry on the explicit computations in the case of a genuinely nonlinear boundary characteristic field (
2.8
) only. The case of a linearly degenerate boundary characteristic field (
2.9
) is analogous (if not simpler), the main difference is that in the definition (
7.8
) of the function
S
(involved in the definition of
\Upsilon
) one has to use the second line rather than the first, and also use estimate (
6.6
) instead of (
6.5
).

7.1.
Definition of the interaction functionals

First, we introduce the following notation: consider a wave
\alpha
connecting the right state
\mathbf{u}_{\alpha}^{+}
with the left state
\mathbf{t}_{j}(\mathbf{u}^{+}_{\alpha},s_{\alpha})
,
j=1,\dots,N
. We term
\vartheta_{\alpha}
the weighted signed strength of
\alpha
, namely

(7.3)

\vartheta_{\alpha}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}s_{\alpha}&j_{\alpha}\geq k\\
A\ s_{\alpha}&j_{\alpha}<k,\\
\end{array}\right.

where
A>1
is a suitable constant, to be determined in what follows. If
\alpha
is a non-physical front between the states
\mathbf{u}^{-}
and
\mathbf{u}^{+}
, then we set
\vartheta_{\alpha}:=|\mathbf{u}^{+}-\mathbf{u}^{-}|
. We define the interaction functional
\Upsilon
by setting

(7.4)

\Upsilon(t):=V(t)+K_{1}Q(t)+K_{1}R(t)+K_{2}S(t)+K_{3}Z(t),

where
K_{1}
,
K_{2}
and
K_{3}
are suitable constants, to be determined in the following, and the quantities
Q(t),R(t)
,
S(t)
and
Z(t)
are defined as

(7.5)

V(t):=|\xi_{k}|+\sum_{\text{waves}}|\vartheta_{\alpha}|,

where
\xi_{k}
is loosely speaking the center component of the boundary layer and is rigorously defined as in cases i),
\dots
,v) in §
5.3
. Also,

(7.6)

Q(t):=\sum_{\text{waves}\;\alpha,\beta}w_{\alpha\beta}|\vartheta_{\alpha}\,\vartheta_{\beta}|,

where the the weight
w_{\alpha\beta}=1
if the wave fronts
\alpha
and
\beta
are approaching, and
w_{\alpha\beta}=0
otherwise.
The definition of “approaching waves” is the same as in the Cauchy problem, see
[
10
, p.137]
, but for the fact that two wave fronts of the
k
-th family are always considered approaching, even if they are both rarefaction wave fronts. We comment on this choice in Remark
7.2
below. We also set

(7.7)

R(t):=|\xi_{k}|\sum_{\text{waves}}|\vartheta_{\beta}|w_{\beta}

where
w_{\beta}=1
if
\beta
belongs to the family
i_{\beta}\leq k
and
w_{\beta}=0
otherwise.
Also,

(7.8)

S(t):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle{\sum_{k-\text{waves}}|s_{\alpha}|\big[\varsigma_{\alpha}(\mathbf{u}_{\alpha},s_{\alpha})\big]^{-}}&\text{if~\eqref{e:gnl}}\\
\displaystyle{\sum_{k-\text{waves}}|s_{\alpha}|\big[\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{u}_{\alpha})\big]^{-}}&\text{if~\eqref{e:lindeg}}\\
\end{array}\right.

In the previous expression,
\varsigma_{\alpha}
is defined as in (
5.4
),
\mathbf{u}_{\alpha}
is the right state of the wave front and
\big[\cdot\big]^{-}
denotes the negative part. Finally,

(7.9)

Z(t)=\mathrm{TotVar}_{[t,+\infty[}\mathbf{u}_{b}^{\nu}.

7.2.
A wave front of the
j
-th family hits the boundary,
j<k

We assume that a wave front
\gamma
with right state
\mathbf{u}^{+}
and left state
\mathbf{t}_{j}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s)
,
j<k
, hits the boundary at time
t
. In §
7.2.1
we consider the case where we use the accurate boundary Riemann solver, see §
5.3
, in §
7.2.2
the case where we use the simplified boundary Riemann solver, see
5.4
.

Remark 7.2
.

Very loosely speaking, the reason why we have to modify the definition of approaching wave fronts in
[
10
, p.137]
and say that two wave fronts of the
k
-th family are
always
approaching (even if they are both rarefaction wave fronts) is the following. Owing to the particular form of our interaction estimate (
6.5
) the center part of the boundary layer, which is parameterized by
\xi_{k}
, must be basically treated as a wave front of the
k
-th family, and this is accounted for in the term
R
defined by (
7.7
). Note that the explicit form of
R
takes into account that the center part of the boundary layer can interact with both rarefaction and shock wave fronts of the
k
-th family. This implies that rarefaction wave fronts of the
k
-th family can interact through the following mechanism: a rarefaction wave front can hit the boundary, get absorbed by the center part of the boundary layer and then interact with any other wave front of the
k
-th family. To take into account this mechanism, we have to add to the functional
Q
the terms accounting for the interaction between rarefaction wave fronts of the
k
-th family. From the technical viewpoint, the fact that wave fronts of the
k
-th family are always approaching yields the term
|\vartheta_{\beta}||s|
at the first line of (
7.22
), which is necessary to establish (
7.26
).

7.2.1.
Accurate Riemann solver

To evaluate
\Delta\Upsilon(t)=\Upsilon(t^{+})-\Upsilon(t^{-})
we separately consider the behavior of
V
,
Q+R
,
S
and
Z
. To this end, we first make a remark about notation.

Remark 7.3
.

As in the previous section, in the following, when writing estimates involving the accurate or simplified boundary Riemann solver, we always use the same notation as in (
5.8
). This implies, in particular, that
s^{\prime}_{k}
does
not
necessarily represent the size of the wave front of the
k
-th family (if any) entering the domain after the interaction. Instead, by separately considering cases i),…,v) in
5.3
we realize that
s^{\prime}_{k}=\xi^{\prime}_{k}+\tilde{s}^{\prime}_{k}
, where
\xi^{\prime}_{k}
is the same as in cases i),…,v) and
\tilde{s}^{\prime}_{k}
is the size of the wave front of the
k
-th family (if any) entering the domain. Note that either
\xi^{\prime}_{k}
or
\tilde{s}^{\prime}_{k}
identically vanish in some of cases i),…,v) in
5.3
. Note furthermore that we always have

(7.10)

|s^{\prime}_{k}|=|\xi^{\prime}_{k}|+|\tilde{s}^{\prime}_{k}|.

To control
\Delta\Upsilon(t)
, first, note that

(7.11)

\Delta V=V(t^{+})-V(t-)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:s},\eqref{e:V},\eqref{e:comprendetutto}}}{{=}}|s^{\prime}_{k}|+\sum_{i=k+1}^{N}|s^{\prime}_{i}|-|\xi_{k}|-A|s|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:nc}}}{{\leq}}|s|(C_{1}-A).

To discuss the behavior of
Q+R
at the interaction we proceed as follows: first, we fix a wave front
\beta
, located at
x_{\beta}>0
, we consider the interaction potentials
Q_{\beta}
and
R_{\beta}
defined by setting

(7.12)

Q_{\beta}(t):=|\vartheta_{\beta}|\sum_{\alpha}w_{\alpha\beta}|\vartheta_{\alpha}|,\qquad R_{\beta}(t):=|\xi_{k}||\vartheta_{\beta}|w_{\beta}.

We now discuss the behavior of
Q_{\beta}+R_{\beta}
at the interaction time
t
.
Note that

(7.13)

Q_{\beta}(t^{-})+R_{\beta}(t^{-})=w_{\gamma\beta}|\vartheta_{\beta}||s|+|\vartheta_{\beta}|\sum_{\alpha\neq\gamma}w_{\alpha\beta}|\vartheta_{\alpha}|+|\xi_{k}||\vartheta_{\beta}|w_{\beta}\geq|\vartheta_{\beta}|\sum_{\alpha\neq\gamma}w_{\alpha\beta}|\vartheta_{\alpha}|+|\xi_{k}||\vartheta_{\beta}|w_{\beta}

and that

(7.14)

Q_{\beta}(t^{+})+R_{\beta}(t^{+})\leq|\vartheta_{\beta}|\sum_{\alpha\neq\gamma}w_{\alpha\beta}|\vartheta_{\alpha}|+|\vartheta_{\beta}|\left[\sum_{i=k+1}^{N}|s^{\prime}_{i}|+|s^{\prime}_{k}|w_{\beta}\right].

Note that the term
|\vartheta_{\beta}||s^{\prime}_{k}|w_{\beta}
in the above expression can contribute to either
Q_{\beta}(t^{+})
(cases i), ii) and iv) in §
5.3
)
or
R_{\beta}(t^{+})
(case v) in §
5.3
), or also to both in case iii) in §
5.3
. In any case we have

Q_{\beta}(t^{+})+R_{\beta}(t^{+})-Q_{\beta}(t^{-})+R_{\beta}(t^{-})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:qbrbm},\eqref{e:qbrbp}}}{{\leq}}|\vartheta_{\beta}|\left[\sum_{i=k+1}^{N}|s^{\prime}_{i}|+(|s^{\prime}_{k}|-|\xi_{k}|)w_{\beta}\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:nc}}}{{\leq}}C_{1}|\vartheta_{\beta}||s|.

Next, we sum up over
\beta
and we point out that
R(t^{+})
may contain a term due to the interaction between the new boundary layer and the new
k
-th wave entering the domain (if any). This can happen only if we are in case iii) discussed in §
5.3
. Note that in this case the strength of the new wave entering the domain is precisely
\bar{s}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})
, where
\mathbf{\hat{u}}
is the same as in (
5.9
). Also, we have

(7.15)

|\xi^{\prime}_{k}|\leq|s^{\prime}_{k}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:nc}}}{{\leq}}|\xi_{k}|+C_{1}|s|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:assumption}}}{{\leq}}\delta+C_{1}\delta=[C_{1}+1]\delta,

which owing to Lemma
6.9
yields

(7.16)

\begin{split}\Delta Q(t)+\Delta R(t)&=Q(t^{+})-Q(t^{-})+R(t^{+})-R(t^{-})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:pallido2},\eqref{e:stimaxip}}}{{\leq}}C_{1}|s|\sum_{\beta}|\vartheta_{\beta}|+C_{12}[1+C_{1}]\delta|s|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:V}}}{{\leq}}C_{1}|s|V(t^{-})+C_{12}[1+C_{1}]\delta|s|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:assumption},\eqref{e:upsilon}}}{{\leq}}[C_{1}+C_{12}+C_{12}C_{1}]|s|\delta.\end{split}

Since the quantities
S
and
Z
are constant at the interaction, we conclude that

(7.17)

\begin{split}\Delta\Upsilon(t)&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:upsilon}}}{{=}}\Delta V(t)+K_{1}\Delta Q(t)+K_{1}\Delta R(t)+K_{2}\Delta S(t)\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:deltav},\eqref{e:deltarq}}}{{\leq}}|s|\left[C_{1}+K_{1}[C_{1}+C_{12}+C_{12}C_{1}]\delta-A\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:uno}}}{{\leq}}-|s|.\end{split}

7.2.2.
Simplified Riemann solver

We consider the case where the interaction is solved with the simplified boundary Riemann solver defined in §
5.4
and we show that
\Delta\Upsilon(t)\leq 0
. As in §
5.4
, we term
\mathbf{u}^{-}
and
\mathbf{u}^{+}
the left and right state of the hitting wave front, respectively. Note that

(7.18)

|\mathbf{u}^{+}-\mathbf{u}^{-}|\leq C_{7}|s|

for a suitable constant
C_{7}>0
only depending on the data
\mathbf{E}
,
\mathbf{A}
,
\mathbf{B}
,
\mathbf{G}
, and on the value
\mathbf{u}^{\ast}
in (
2.7
).
We recall the construction in §
5.4
and conclude in particular that after the interaction the only front entering the domain is a non-physical front of strength
|\mathbf{u}^{+}-\mathbf{u}^{-}|
. We have

\Delta V(t)=-A|s|+|\mathbf{u}^{+}-\mathbf{u}^{-}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:cisette}}}{{\leq}}(-A+C_{7})|s|

and

\Delta Q(t)\leq|\mathbf{u}^{+}-\mathbf{u}^{-}|\sum_{\text{waves}}|\vartheta_{\alpha}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:assumption},\eqref{e:cisette}}}{{\leq}}C_{7}\delta|s|,\qquad\Delta R(t)\leq-A|\xi_{k}||s|,\qquad\Delta S(t)=\Delta Z(t)=0

and by recalling (
7.4
) this yields

(7.19)

\Delta\Upsilon(t)\leq\big[-A+C_{7}(1+K_{1}\delta)\big]|s|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:uno}}}{{\leq}}-|s|\leq 0.

7.3.
A wave front of the
k
-th family hits the boundary

We assume that a wave front
\gamma
with right state
\mathbf{u}^{+}
and left state
\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s)
hits the boundary at time
t
. In §
7.3.1
we consider the case where we use the accurate boundary Riemann solver, see §
5.3
, in §
7.3.2
the case where we use the simplified boundary Riemann solver, see
5.4
. In this paragraph we use the shorthand notation
\varsigma_{k}
for the quantity defined by (
5.4
).

7.3.1.
Accurate boundary Riemann solver

We have

(7.20)

\begin{split}\Delta V(t)&=V(t^{+})-V(t-)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:V}}}{{=}}|s^{\prime}_{k}|+\sum_{i=k+1}^{N}|s^{\prime}_{i}|-|\xi_{k}|-|s_{k}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:me}}}{{\leq}}C_{2}|s|\Big([\varsigma_{k}]^{-}+|\xi_{k}|\Big).\end{split}

Next, we proceed as in the previous paragraph: we fix a wave front
\beta
of the
i_{\beta}
-th family located at
x_{\beta}>0
and we consider the same functionals
Q_{\beta}
and
R_{\beta}
as in (
7.12
). We first focus on the case
i_{\beta}>k
: since
w_{\beta}=0
then
R_{\beta}(t^{-})=R_{\beta}(t^{+})=0
. Also,

Q_{\beta}(t^{-})=|\vartheta_{\beta}|\sum_{\alpha}w_{\alpha\beta}|\vartheta_{\alpha}|,\qquad Q_{\beta}(t^{+})\leq|\vartheta_{\beta}|\sum_{\alpha}w_{\alpha\beta}|\vartheta_{\alpha}|+|\vartheta_{\beta}|\sum_{i=k+1}^{N}|s^{\prime}_{i}|\quad\text{if}\;i_{\beta}>k,

which yields

(7.21)

\Delta Q_{\beta}(t)+\Delta R_{\beta}(t)\leq|\vartheta_{\beta}|\sum_{i=k}^{N}|s^{\prime}_{i}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:me}}}{{\leq}}C_{2}|\vartheta_{\beta}||s|\Big([\varsigma_{k}]^{-}+|\xi_{k}|\Big).

Next, we assume
i_{\beta}\leq k
, recall that according to our definition two wave fronts of the
k
-th family are always approaching and arrive at

(7.22)

\begin{split}&Q_{\beta}(t^{-})+R_{\beta}(t^{-})=|\vartheta_{\beta}|\sum_{\alpha\neq\gamma}w_{\alpha\beta}|\vartheta_{\alpha}|+|\vartheta_{\beta}||s|+|\vartheta_{\beta}||\xi_{k}|,\\
&Q_{\beta}(t^{+})+R_{\beta}(t^{+})=|\vartheta_{\beta}|\sum_{\alpha\neq\gamma}w_{\alpha\beta}|\vartheta_{\alpha}|+|\vartheta_{\beta}|\left(\sum_{i=k+1}^{N}|s^{\prime}_{i}|+|s^{\prime}_{k}|\right)\qquad\text{if $i_{\beta}\leq k$},\end{split}

and hence

(7.23)

\Delta Q_{\beta}(t)+\Delta R_{\beta}(t)=|\vartheta_{\beta}|\left(\sum_{i=k}^{N}|s^{\prime}_{i}|+|s^{\prime}_{k}|-|s|-|\xi_{k}|\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:me}}}{{\leq}}C_{2}|\vartheta_{\beta}||s|\Big([\varsigma_{k}]^{-}+|\xi_{k}|\Big).

We now point out that

R(t^{-})=|\xi_{k}||s|+\sum_{\beta}R_{\beta}(t)

and we observe that the only case where after the interaction we have both

\xi^{\prime}_{k}\neq 0
and there is wave front entering the domain is if we are in case iii) in §
5.3
. We can then apply Lemma
6.8
and conclude that the contribution of this specific term to the functional
R(t^{+})
is controlled by
C_{8}|\xi^{\prime}_{k}||s|(|\xi_{k}|+[\varsigma_{k}]^{-})
. By using estimate (
7.15
) we then
arrive at

(7.24)

\begin{split}\Delta Q(t)&+\Delta R(t)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:rarepiccola},\eqref{e:stimaxip}}}{{=}}-|\xi_{k}||s|+2C_{8}\delta|s|(|\xi_{k}|+[\varsigma_{k}]^{-})+\sum_{\beta}\Delta Q_{\beta}(t)+\Delta R_{\beta}(t)\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:dqdr1},\eqref{e:dqdr2}}}{{\leq}}-|\xi_{k}||s|+|s|\Big([\varsigma_{k}]^{-}+|\xi_{k}|\Big)\left(2C_{8}\delta+C_{2}\sum_{\beta}|\vartheta_{\beta}|\right)\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:upsilon},\eqref{e:assumption}}}{{\leq}}-|\xi_{k}||s|+[2C_{8}+C_{2}]|s|\Big([\varsigma_{k}]^{-}+|\xi_{k}|\Big)\delta=[2C_{8}+C_{2}]\delta|s|[\varsigma_{k}]^{-}+|s||\xi_{k}|([2C_{8}+C_{2}]\delta-1).\end{split}

Next, we point out that

(7.25)

S(t^{-})=\sum_{k-\text{wave}\neq\gamma}|s_{\alpha}|\big[\varsigma_{\alpha}\big]^{-}+|s|[\varsigma_{k}]^{-},\quad S(t^{+})=\sum_{k-\text{wave}\neq\gamma}|s_{\alpha}|\big[\varsigma_{\alpha}\big]^{-}\implies\Delta S(t)=-|s|\big[\varsigma_{k}\big]^{-}

which yields (since the quantity
Z
defined by (
7.9
) is constant at the interaction)

(7.26)

\begin{split}\Delta\Upsilon(t)&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:upsilon}}}{{=}}\Delta V(t)+K_{1}\Delta Q(t)+K_{1}\Delta R(t)+K_{2}\Delta S(t)\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:c:tv},\eqref{e:cqr},\eqref{e:cds}}}{{\leq}}|s|[\varsigma_{k}]^{-}[C_{2}+K_{1}[2C_{8}+C_{2}]\delta-K_{2}]+|s||\xi_{k}|[C_{2}+K_{1}([2C_{8}+C_{2}]\delta-1)]\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:due}}}{{\leq}}-|s|([\varsigma_{k}]^{-}+|\xi_{k}|).\end{split}

7.3.2.
Simplified boundary Riemann solver

We recall the construction in §
5.4
and the definition (
5.9
) of
\mathbf{\hat{u}}
and by increasing, if needed, the value of
C_{7}
we obtain

(7.27)

|\mathbf{u}^{+}-\mathbf{\hat{u}}|\leq C_{7}\sum_{i=k+1}^{N}|s^{\prime}_{i}|

We can then repeat the same computations as in §
7.3
replacing
\sum_{i=k+1}^{N}|s^{\prime}_{i}|
with
C_{7}\sum_{i=k+1}^{N}|s_{i}|
. By (
1.11
), we obtain
\Delta\Upsilon(t)\leq 0.

7.4.
The boundary datum
\mathbf{u}_{b}^{\varepsilon}
has a jump discontinuity

Assume that the piecewise constant function
\mathbf{u}^{\varepsilon}_{b}
experiences a jump discontinuity at
t
. We set

\mathbf{u}_{b}^{-}:=\lim_{s\to t^{-}}\mathbf{u}_{b}^{\varepsilon}(s),\quad\mathbf{u}_{b}^{+}:=\lim_{s\to t^{+}}\mathbf{u}_{b}^{\varepsilon}(s),

which yields

(7.28)

\Delta Z(t)=Z(t^{+})-Z(t^{-})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:Z}}}{{=}}-|\mathbf{u}_{b}^{+}-\mathbf{u}_{b}^{-}|.

We also set
\mathbf{\bar{u}_{\ast}}:=\lim_{x\to 0^{+}}\mathbf{u}^{\varepsilon}(t,x)
, where
\mathbf{u}^{\varepsilon}
is the
\varepsilon
-wave front-tracking approximation.
We recall the analysis in§
5.3
and for simplicity assume (
2.11
) (the analysis in
Cases B)
and
C)
in Hypothesis
5
is analogous). We conclude that

(7.29)

\boldsymbol{\beta}(\boldsymbol{\phi}(\mathbf{\bar{u}_{\ast}},\xi_{\ell+1},\dots,\xi_{k}),\mathbf{u}^{-}_{b})=\mathbf{0}_{N-\ell},\quad\boldsymbol{\beta}(\boldsymbol{\phi}(\cdot,\xi^{\prime}_{\ell+1},\dots,\xi^{\prime}_{k-1},s^{\prime}_{k})\circ\mathbf{t}_{k+1}(\cdot,s^{\prime}_{k+1})\dots\circ\mathbf{t}_{N}(\mathbf{\bar{u}}_{\ast},s^{\prime}_{N}),\mathbf{u}^{+}_{b})=\mathbf{0}_{N-\ell}.

By the Lipschitz continuity of the implicit function we conclude that

(7.30)

\sum_{i=\ell+1}^{k-1}|\xi^{\prime}_{i}-\xi_{i}|+|s^{\prime}_{k}-\xi_{k}|+\sum_{j=k+1}^{N}|s^{\prime}_{j}|\leq C_{6}|\mathbf{u}_{b}^{+}-\mathbf{u}_{b}^{-}|

for a suitable constant
C_{6}>0
.
This yields

(7.31)

\Delta V(t)=V(t^{+})-V(t^{-})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:V},\eqref{e:jd1}}}{{\leq}}C_{6}|\mathbf{u}_{b}^{+}-\mathbf{u}_{b}^{-}|.

In the sequel we need the following lemma.

Lemma 7.1
.

Under (
7.29
), assume that

(7.32)

\xi_{k}\leq\underline{s}(\mathbf{\bar{u}}_{\ast})\;\text{if}\;\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\bar{u}}_{\ast})\leq 0,\qquad\xi_{k}=0\;\text{if}\;\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\bar{u}}_{\ast})>0.

Let
\mathbf{\hat{u}}
be as in (
5.9
) with
\mathbf{\bar{u}_{\ast}}
in place of
\mathbf{u}^{+}
and assume that

(7.33)

\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})\geq 0,\quad s^{\prime}_{k}<\bar{s}(\mathbf{\hat{u}}).

Then

(7.34)

|\bar{s}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})|\leq C_{11}|\mathbf{u}_{b}^{+}-\mathbf{u}_{b}^{-}|.

for a suitable constant
C_{11}>0
.

Proof.

We can follow the proof of Lemma
6.9
and replace
\mathbf{t}_{j}(\mathbf{u}^{+},s_{j})
with
\mathbf{\bar{u}}
, and
s_{j}
with
|\mathbf{u}_{b}^{+}-\mathbf{u}_{b}^{-}|
. The details are omitted.
∎

The reason why we need the above lemma is the following: we want to evaluate
\Delta Q(t)+\Delta R(t)
. To this end, we recall the analysis in §
5.3
and we point out that after the interaction the term
R
may contain a contribution in the form
|\xi^{\prime}_{k}||\bar{s}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})|
, where
\xi^{\prime}_{k}
is defined as in §
5.3
and
\mathbf{\hat{u}}
is as in (
5.9
), but only if we are in case iii) in §
5.3
. We recall that in this case
\xi^{\prime}_{k}=s^{\prime}_{k}-\bar{s}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})
. By applying Lemma
7.1
we conclude that

(7.35)

\begin{split}|\xi^{\prime}_{k}||\bar{s}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})|&=|s^{\prime}_{k}-\bar{s}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})||\bar{s}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bar{s}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})<0\;\text{in case iii)}}}{{\leq}}|s^{\prime}_{k}||\bar{s}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:jd1},\eqref{e:pallido}}}{{\leq}}[|\xi_{k}|+C_{6}||\mathbf{u}_{b}^{+}-\mathbf{u}_{b}^{-}|]C_{11}|\mathbf{u}_{b}^{+}-\mathbf{u}_{b}^{-}|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:assumption}}}{{\leq}}[1+C_{6}]C_{11}\delta|\mathbf{u}_{b}^{+}-\mathbf{u}_{b}^{-}|.\end{split}

Wrapping up, we have

(7.36)

\begin{split}\Delta Q(t)+\Delta R(t)&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:Q},\eqref{e:R}}}{{\leq}}\left(\sum_{j=k+1}^{N}|s^{\prime}_{j}|\right)\sum_{\beta}|\vartheta_{\beta}|+|s^{\prime}_{k}|\sum_{\beta}w_{\beta}|\vartheta_{\beta}|+|\xi^{\prime}_{k}||\bar{s}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})|-|\xi_{k}|\sum_{\beta}w_{\beta}|\vartheta_{\beta}|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:jd1},\eqref{e:brina}}}{{\leq}}2C_{6}|\mathbf{u}_{b}^{+}-\mathbf{u}_{b}^{-}|\sum_{\beta}|\vartheta_{\beta}|+[1+C_{6}]C_{11}\delta|\mathbf{u}_{b}^{+}-\mathbf{u}_{b}^{-}|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:assumption}}}{{\leq}}\big(2C_{6}+[1+C_{6}]C_{11}\big)\delta|\mathbf{u}_{b}^{+}-\mathbf{u}_{b}^{-}|\end{split}

Since
\Delta S(t)=0
(because new wave fronts of the
k
-th family that enter the domain, if any, have positive speed)
we conclude that

(7.37)

\begin{split}\Delta\Upsilon(t)&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:upsilon}}}{{=}}\Delta V(t)+K_{1}\Delta Q(t)+K_{1}\Delta R(t)+K_{3}\Delta Z(t)\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:jdz},\eqref{e:jdv},\eqref{e:jdqr}}}{{=}}|\mathbf{u}_{b}^{+}-\mathbf{u}_{b}^{-}|\Big(C_{6}+K_{1}\delta\big(2C_{6}+[1+C_{6}]C_{11}\big)-K_{3}\Big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:4}}}{{\leq}}-|\mathbf{u}_{b}^{+}-\mathbf{u}_{b}^{-}|.\end{split}

7.5.
Two wave fronts interacts inside the domain

In this paragraph we assume that at time
t
two wave fronts
\alpha
and a
\beta
intersect. We assume that
\alpha
and
\beta
belong to the family

i_{\alpha}
and
i_{\beta}
, respectively, and with a slight abuse of notation we say that if
\alpha
is a non-physical wave front then
i_{\alpha}=N+1
. We also term
\mathbf{u}^{+}_{\alpha}
and
\mathbf{u}^{+}_{\beta}
the right state of
\alpha
and
\beta
, respectively, and by
\mathbf{t}_{i_{\alpha}}(\mathbf{u}_{\alpha}^{+},s_{\alpha})
and
\mathbf{t}_{i_{\beta}}(\mathbf{u}_{\beta}^{+},s_{\beta})
the left state. If
\alpha
is a non-physical wave front we set
s_{\alpha}:=|\mathbf{u}^{+}_{\alpha}-\mathbf{u}^{-}_{\alpha}|
, where
\mathbf{u}^{-}_{\alpha}
is the left state. We also term
s^{\prime}_{\alpha}
and
s^{\prime}_{\beta}
the sizes of the
i_{\alpha}
and
i_{\beta}
wave front (if any) outgoing the interaction.

The exposition is organized as follows: in §
7.5.1
we give some preliminary results, in §
7.5.2
we assume that the interaction is solved by the accurate Riemann solver, see §
5.2.1
, in §
7.5.3
we assume that the interaction is solved by the simplified Riemann solver, see §
5.2.2
, and finally in §
7.5.4
we discuss the interaction between a physical and a non-physical wave front.

7.5.1.
Preliminary results

We first recall
[
10
, eq (7.31),(732) p. 133]
and
[
10
, eq (7.33),(7.34) p. 134]
and conclude
18
18

18

Note that, strictly speaking, estimates (7.31)-(7.34) in
[
10
]
involve the wave strengths along the admissible wave fan curves of
right
states
\boldsymbol{m}_{i}
, rather than the admissible wave fan curves of
left
states
\mathbf{t}_{i}
. See also footnote
5
at page
5
and Remark
5.1
at page
5.1
. However, owing to the relation (
5.7
), these estimates straightforwardly extend to the strengths of the waves along the curves
\mathbf{t}_{i}
,
i=1,\dots,N
, that is the strengths involved in the definition of the functionals in §
7.1
.

that there is a constant
C_{5}>0
only depending on
\mathbf{E}
,
\mathbf{A}
and
\mathbf{u}^{\ast}
such that

(7.38)

|s^{\prime}_{\alpha}-s_{\alpha}|+|s^{\prime}_{\beta}-s_{\beta}|+\sum_{j\neq i_{\alpha},i_{\beta}}|s^{\prime}_{j}|\leq C_{5}|s_{\alpha}s_{\beta}|\quad\text{for $i_{\alpha},i_{\beta}\in\{1,\dots,N+1\}$, $i_{\alpha}\neq i_{\beta}$}

and

(7.39)

|s^{\prime}_{\alpha}-(s_{\alpha}+s_{\beta})|+\sum_{j\neq i_{\alpha}}|s^{\prime}_{j}|\leq C_{5}|s_{\alpha}s_{\beta}|\quad\text{for $i_{\alpha},i_{\beta}\in\{1,\dots,N+1\}$, $i_{\alpha}=i_{\beta}$}.

Next, we control the change in the quantity defined by (
5.4
) at the interaction.

Lemma 7.2
.

Assume
i_{\alpha}=k
and let
\mathbf{u}_{\alpha}
and
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{\alpha}
denote the right state of the wave front of family
i_{\alpha}
before and after the interaction, respectively. Then there are constants
\nu>0
and
C_{3}>0
only depending on
\mathbf{E}
,
\mathbf{A}
and
\mathbf{u}^{\ast}
such that, if
|s_{\alpha}|\leq\nu
and
|s_{\beta}|\leq\nu
then

(7.40)

|-\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}_{\alpha},s_{\alpha})+\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{\alpha},s^{\prime}_{\alpha})|\leq C_{3}|s_{\beta}|,

provided
\varsigma_{k}
is the same as in (
5.4
).

Proof of Lemma
7.2
.

It suffices to point out that the function
\varsigma_{k}
defined by (
5.4
) is Lipschitz continuous with respect to both
\mathbf{\widetilde{u}}
and
s_{k}
, and that owing to (
7.38
) and (
7.39
)

|\mathbf{u}_{\alpha}-\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{\alpha}|\leq\mathcal{O}(1)|s_{\beta}|+\mathcal{O}(1)|s_{\alpha}s_{\beta}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:assumption}}}{{\leq}}\mathcal{O}(1)|s_{\beta}|(1+\delta).

∎

Note that an elementary computation shows that (
7.40
) implies

(7.41)

\Big|[\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}_{\alpha},s_{\alpha})]^{-}-[\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{\alpha},s^{\prime}_{\alpha})]^{-}\Big|\leq C_{3}|s_{\beta}|.

We now recall (
1.7
),(
2.7
) and (
7.1
), and point out that by our construction of the
\varepsilon
-wave front-tracking approximation
\mathbf{u}_{\varepsilon}
we have
\lim_{x\to+\infty}\mathbf{u}_{\varepsilon}(t,x)=\lim_{x\to+\infty}\mathbf{u}_{0}(x)
for every
t\geq 0
. We conclude that if
\delta^{\ast}\leq\delta
then
there is a constant
C_{4}
only depending on
\mathbf{E}
,
\mathbf{A}
and
\mathbf{u}^{\ast}
such that

(7.42)

\sup_{\text{$k$-wave}}{[\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}_{\alpha},s_{\alpha})]^{-}}\leq\sup_{\text{$k$-wave}}{|\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}_{\alpha},s_{\alpha})|}\leq C_{4}\delta.

7.5.2.
Accurate Riemann solver

We consider the case that the interaction between the wave fronts
\alpha
and
\beta
is solved by the accurate Riemann solver.
By applying either (
7.38
) or (
7.39
) we conclude that

(7.43)

\Delta V(t)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:V}}}{{\leq}}AC_{5}|s_{\alpha}s_{\beta}|

To control
\Delta Q(t)
we basically proceed as in
[
10
, p.137]
, the only difference is that our definition of approaching waves is slightly different. More precisely, according to our definition two wave fronts of the
k
-th family are always approaching, even if they are both rarefaction wave fronts.
We recall that rarefaction fronts of the same family of incoming wave-fronts are never partitioned (see property
[
10
, (P)]
) and we realize that the difference with the analysis in
[
10
, p.137]
occurs when
i_{\alpha}\neq k\neq i_{\beta}
and some rarefaction wave fronts of the
k
-th family are generated at the interaction. Let us denote by
m
the number of the
outgoing rarefaction wave fronts of the
k
-th family, then the increment of
Q
due to the interaction among these waves can be controlled by

\underbrace{\left(\frac{AC_{5}|s_{\alpha}s_{\beta}|}{m}\right)}_{\text{maximal strength of the single wave front}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\left(\frac{AC_{5}|s_{\alpha}s_{\beta}|}{m}\right)\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\underbrace{\sum_{h=1}^{m-1}h}_{\text{number of possible interactions}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\leq\frac{A^{2}C_{5}^{2}|s_{\alpha}s_{\beta}|^{2}}{m^{2}}\frac{m(m-1)}{2}\!\!\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:s},\eqref{e:V},\eqref{e:upsilon},\eqref{e:assumption}}}{{\leq}}\!\!A^{2}C^{2}_{5}\delta^{2}|s_{\alpha}s_{\beta}|.

By recalling (
7.38
) and (
7.39
) this yields

(7.44)

\Delta Q(t)\leq-|\vartheta_{\alpha}\vartheta_{\beta}|+AC_{5}|s_{\alpha}s_{\beta}|\sum_{\omega\neq\alpha,\beta}|\vartheta_{\omega}|+A^{2}C^{2}_{5}\delta^{2}|s_{\alpha}s_{\beta}|\!\!\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:assumption},\eqref{e:upsilon},\eqref{e:V}}}{{\leq}}\!\!-|\vartheta_{\alpha}\vartheta_{\beta}|+AC_{5}\delta(1+\delta AC_{5})|s_{\alpha}s_{\beta}|

and that

(7.45)

\Delta R(t)\leq A|\xi_{k}|C_{5}|s_{\alpha}s_{\beta}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:assumption},\eqref{e:upsilon},\eqref{e:V}}}{{\leq}}AC_{5}\delta|s_{\alpha}s_{\beta}|

We now control
\Delta S(t):
if
i_{\alpha}\neq k
and
i_{\beta}\neq k
, then we combine (
7.42
) with either (
7.38
) or (
7.39
) and conclude that

(7.46)

\Delta S(t)\leq C_{4}C_{5}\delta|s_{\alpha}s_{\beta}|\qquad\text{if}\;i_{\alpha}\neq k,\;i_{\beta}\neq k.

If
i_{\alpha}=k
and
i_{\beta}\neq k
, then

(7.47)

\begin{split}&\Delta S(t)\!\!=\!\!|s^{\prime}_{\alpha}|[\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{\alpha},s^{\prime}_{\alpha})]^{-}\!\!-\!\!|s_{\alpha}|[\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}_{\alpha},s_{\alpha})]^{-}\!\!\!=\!\![\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{\alpha},s^{\prime}_{\alpha})]^{-}\big(|s^{\prime}_{\alpha}|\!\!-\!\!|s_{\alpha}|\big)\!+\!|s_{\alpha}|\big([\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{\alpha},s^{\prime}_{\alpha})]^{-}-[\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}_{\alpha},s_{\alpha})]^{-}\big)\\
&\!\!\!\!\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:msigma},\eqref{e:iebressan}}}{{\leq}}\!\!\!\!C_{4}C_{5}\delta|s_{\alpha}||s_{\beta}|+|s_{\alpha}|\big([\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{\alpha},s^{\prime}_{\alpha})]^{-}-[\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}_{\alpha},s_{\alpha})]^{-}\big)\!\!\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:dadim3bis}}}{{\leq}}\!\!C_{4}C_{5}\delta|s_{\alpha}s_{\beta}|+C_{3}|s_{\alpha}s_{\beta}|\end{split}

If
i_{\alpha}=k
and
i_{\beta}=k
, then

(7.48)

\begin{split}\Delta S(t)&=|s^{\prime}_{\alpha}|[\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{\alpha},s^{\prime}_{\alpha})]^{-}-|s_{\alpha}|[\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}_{\alpha},s_{\alpha})]^{-}-|s_{\beta}|[\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}_{\beta},s_{\beta})]^{-}\\
&=[\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{\alpha},s^{\prime}_{\alpha})]^{-}\big(|s^{\prime}_{\alpha}|-|s_{\alpha}|-|s_{\beta}|\big)+\big(|s_{\alpha}|+|s_{\beta}|\big)[\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{\alpha},s^{\prime}_{\alpha})]^{-}-|s_{\alpha}|[\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}_{\alpha},s_{\alpha})]^{-}-|s_{\beta}|[\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}_{\beta},s_{\beta})]^{-}\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:msigma},\eqref{e:iebressan2}}}{{\leq}}C_{4}C_{5}\delta|s_{\alpha}||s_{\beta}|+|s_{\alpha}|\big([\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{\alpha},s^{\prime}_{\alpha})]^{-}-[\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}_{\alpha},s_{\alpha})]^{-}\big)+|s_{\beta}|\big([\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{\alpha},s^{\prime}_{\alpha})]^{-}-[\varsigma_{k}(\mathbf{u}_{\beta},s_{\beta})]^{-}\big)\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:dadim3bis}}}{{\leq}}C_{4}C_{5}\delta|s_{\alpha}s_{\beta}|+2C_{3}|s_{\alpha}s_{\beta}|\end{split}

Since
\Delta Z(t)=0
we conclude that

(7.49)

\begin{split}\Delta\Upsilon(t)&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:upsilon}}}{{=}}\Delta V(t)+K_{1}\Delta Q(t)+K_{1}\Delta R(t)+K_{2}\Delta S(t)\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:idv},\eqref{e:idq},\eqref{e:deltar}}}{{\leq}}|s_{\alpha}s_{\beta}|\Big[AC_{5}+\delta K_{1}AC_{5}(2+\delta AC_{5})\Big]-K_{1}|\vartheta_{\alpha}\vartheta_{\beta}|+K_{2}\Delta S(t)\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:deltas1},\eqref{e:deltas2},\eqref{e:deltas3}}}{{\leq}}|s_{\alpha}s_{\beta}|\Big[AC_{5}+\delta K_{1}AC_{5}(2+\delta AC_{5})+K_{2}C_{4}C_{5}\delta+2K_{2}C_{3}\Big]-K_{1}|\vartheta_{\alpha}\vartheta_{\beta}|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:s},\ A>1}}{{\leq}}|s_{\alpha}s_{\beta}|\Big(AC_{5}-K_{1}+2K_{1}A\delta C_{5}+K_{1}A^{2}C_{5}^{2}\delta^{2}+K_{2}C_{4}C_{5}\delta+2K_{2}C_{3}\Big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:tre}}}{{\leq}}-|s_{\alpha}s_{\beta}|.\end{split}

7.5.3.
Simplified Riemann solver

The analysis is analogous to the one in §
7.5.2
, but simpler. We have
\Delta V(t)\leq C_{5}|s_{\alpha}s_{\beta}|
,
\Delta Q(t)\leq-|s_{\alpha}s_{\beta}|+AC_{5}\delta|s_{\alpha}s_{\beta}|
,
\Delta R(t)=0
. We also have (
7.46
),(
7.47
) and (
7.48
) and by following (
7.49
) we arrive at
\Delta\Upsilon(t)\leq-|s_{\alpha}s_{\beta}|
.

7.5.4.
Interaction between a physical and a non-physical front

In this case we have
\Delta V(t)\leq C_{5}|s_{\alpha}s_{\beta}|
,
\Delta Q(t)=-|s_{\alpha}s_{\beta}|
,
\Delta R(t)=0
. We also have either
\Delta S(t)=0
(if the physical front is not a
k
-wave) or (
7.47
) (if the physical front is a
k
-wave) and by following (
7.49
) we arrive at
\Delta\Upsilon(t)\leq-|s_{\alpha}s_{\beta}|
.

8.
Convergence proof

In this section we establish (
1.8
) for some function
\mathbf{v}
such that
