\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{k}(\mathbf{\underline{u}}(t),\xi_{k}(t))=\mathbf{b}_{k}(\mathbf{\underline{u}}(t),\xi_{k}(t))
. Owing to Lemma
4.5
, the orbit
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{0}
converges to
(\mathbf{\underline{u}}(t),\mathbf{0}_{N-h})
as
y\to+\infty
. Wrapping up, we conclude that the solution of the Cauchy problem obtained by coupling (
3.2
) and
\boldsymbol{\omega}(0)
in (
4.48
) converges to
(\mathbf{\underline{u}}(t),\mathbf{0}_{N-h})
as
y\to+\infty
. Recalling that the function
\mathbf{a}
in (
3.2
) is given by (
3.3
), this yields the existence of a boundary layer
\mathbf{w}
satisfying (
4.7
) with asymptotic state

\mathbf{\underline{u}}(t)
.

Step 2:
we establish property i) in Definition (
1.1
). We have

\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{\underline{v}}(t))=\mathbf{f}\circ\mathbf{v}\circ\mathbf{\underline{u}}(t)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:tracciainterna}}}{{=}}\lim_{m\to+\infty}\mathbf{f}\circ\mathbf{v}\circ\mathbf{\bar{u}}_{m}(t)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:flupointwise},\eqref{e:baruemme}}}{{=}}\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{\bar{v}}(t)),

and since
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\bar{u}}(t))\leq 0
by (
9.40
) the
0
-speed shock between
\mathbf{\underline{u}}
(on the left) and
\mathbf{\bar{u}}(t)
(on the right) is Lax admissible. This concludes the proof of Lemma
9.10
.
∎

9.6.
Case of a linearly degenerate case characteristic field

We now establish the proof of Theorem
9.1
in the case of a linearly degenerate boundary characteristic field (
2.9
). The proof follows the same outline as in the case of a genuinely nonlinear characteristic field, so we only discuss the points where the argument is different.

9.6.1.
Proof of Lemma
9.2
(linearly degenerate
k
-th characteristic field)

The argument is very similar to the one in the case of a genuinely nonlinear characteristic field, so we only provide a sketch. First, we recall §
5.3.2
and also we point out that instead of (
9.4
) we have the inequality

(9.47)

|\mathbf{f}\circ\mathbf{v}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})-\mathbf{f}\circ\mathbf{v}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}},s))|\leq\mathcal{O}(1)|s||\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\widetilde{u}})|.

Next, we consider the same functional
\Lambda
as in (
9.5
). We recall that now the functional
S
in
\Upsilon
is given by the second line of (
7.8
). We now discuss
Cases I,
\dots
,III
in the proof of Lemma
9.2
.

Case I:
it suffices to establish (
9.7
). To this end, we term
\mathbf{u}^{-}
and
\mathbf{u}^{+}
the left and right state of the hitting wave of the
j
-th family,
j<k
, and by
\mathbf{\hat{u}}
the same state as in (
5.9
). Next, we point out that there are two possibilities:

•

if
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})>0
by following the same computations done in (
9.8
) for cases i),
\dots
, iii) in §
5.3
, we arrive at

\Delta F(\tau)\leq\mathcal{O}(1)|s_{j}|+|\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{v}(\mathbf{\hat{u}}))-\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{v}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\hat{u}},s_{k})))|

and the second term at the right-hand side of the above expression can be controlled by arguing as in (
9.9
).

•

if
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})\leq 0
there is no wave front of the
k
-th family entering the domain after the interaction and hence the last term at the right hand side of (
9.8
) is not there, and in this way we arrive at (
9.7
).

Case II:
we term
\mathbf{u}^{-}
and
\mathbf{u}^{+}
the left and right state of the hitting wave front of the
k
-th family, and
\mathbf{\hat{u}}
the same state as in (
5.9
). Assume that we have shown that

(9.48)

\Delta F(\tau)\leq\mathcal{O}(1)|s_{k}|\big(|\xi_{k}|+[\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+})]^{-}\big);

then we can choose the constant
K_{5}
in such a way that the function
\Lambda
is decreasing at
\tau
. To establish (
9.48
) we first assume that
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})>0
and we point out that

\begin{split}\Delta F(\tau)&\leq|\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{v}(\mathbf{u}^{-}))-\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{v}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\hat{u}},s^{\prime}_{k})))|\\
&\leq|\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{v}(\mathbf{u}^{-}))-\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{v}(\mathbf{u}^{+}))|+|\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{v}(\mathbf{u}^{+}))-\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{v}(\mathbf{\hat{u}}))|+|\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{v}(\mathbf{\hat{u}}))-\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{v}(\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\hat{u}},s^{\prime}_{k})))|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:melindeg},\eqref{e:dflux2}}}{{\leq}}[\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+})]^{-}|s_{k}|+\mathcal{O}(1)|s_{k}||\xi_{k}|+|\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})||s^{\prime}_{k}|\\
&\leq[\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+})]^{-}|s_{k}|+\mathcal{O}(1)|s_{k}||\xi_{k}|+\delta([\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})]^{+}-[\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+})]^{+})\\
&\leq[\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+})]^{-}|s_{k}|+\mathcal{O}(1)|s_{k}||\xi_{k}|+\delta\mathcal{O}(1)|\mathbf{\hat{u}}-\mathbf{u}^{+}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:melindeg}}}{{\leq}}[\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{u}^{+})]^{-}|s_{k}|+\mathcal{O}(1)|s_{k}||\xi_{k}|+\delta|s_{k}||\xi_{k}|,\end{split}

that is (
9.48
). If
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})\leq 0
the estimate is actually simpler because we do not have the last term at the right hand side of the above expression since there is no
k
-th wave front entering the domain after the interaction.

Case III:
as in the proof of Lemma
9.2
we use the following notation:

•

\mathbf{u}_{bm}^{+}
and
\mathbf{u}_{bm}^{-}
are the right and left limits
\mathbf{u}^{\varepsilon_{m}}_{b}(\tau^{+})
and
\mathbf{u}^{\varepsilon_{m}}_{b}(\tau^{-})
, respectively;

•

\mathbf{\bar{u}}^{-}
and
\mathbf{\bar{u}}^{+}
are the traces of
\mathbf{u}_{m}(t,\cdot)
for
t=\tau^{-}
and
t=\tau^{+}
, respectively;

•

\mathbf{\hat{u}}
is the same state as in (
5.9
).

As in the genuinely nonlinear case, it suffices to establish (
9.17
). We recall that if
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})\leq 0
then there is no wave front of the
k
-th family entering the domain immediately after time
\tau
. This yields
\mathbf{\bar{u}}^{+}=\mathbf{\hat{u}}
and hence

\Delta F(\tau)\leq|\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{v}(\mathbf{\hat{u}}))-\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{v}(\mathbf{u}^{-}))|\leq\mathcal{O}(1)\sum_{j=k+1}^{N}|s^{\prime}_{j}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:jd1}}}{{\leq}}\mathcal{O}(1)|\mathbf{u}_{bm}^{+}-\mathbf{u}^{-}_{bm}|,

that is (
9.7
). We now consider the case
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})>0
and assume that there is a wave-front of the
k
-th family with strength
s_{k}
entering the domain after the interaction. We point out that

(9.49)

\begin{split}\Delta F(\tau)&\leq|\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{v}(\mathbf{\hat{u}}))-\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{v}(\mathbf{\bar{u}}^{-}))|+|\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{v}(\mathbf{\hat{u}}))-\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{v}(\underbrace{\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\hat{u}},s_{k})}_{=\mathbf{\bar{u}}^{+}})|\!\!\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:jd1},\eqref{e:dflux2}}}{{\leq}}\!\!\mathcal{O}(1)|\mathbf{u}_{bm}^{+}-\mathbf{u}^{-}_{bm}|+\mathcal{O}(1)|s_{k}||\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})|\end{split}

If
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\bar{u}}^{-})\leq 0
then we can control the last term at the right-hand side of (
9.49
) as follows:

\begin{split}|s_{k}||\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})|=|s_{k}|[\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})]^{+}\leq|s_{k}||\big([\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\hat{u}})]^{+}-[\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\bar{u}}^{-})]^{+}|\big)\leq\mathcal{O}(1)|s_{k}||\mathbf{\hat{u}}-\mathbf{\bar{u}}^{-}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:jd1}}}{{\leq}}\mathcal{O}(1)|s_{k}||\mathbf{u}_{bm}^{+}-\mathbf{u}^{-}_{bm}|\end{split}

and by plugging the above inequality into (
9.49
) we arrive at (
9.7
). If
\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\bar{u}}^{-})>0
then we can apply (
4.60
) with
\xi_{k}
and
\mathbf{\bar{u}}^{-}
in place of
\xi^{\prime}_{k}
and
\mathbf{\bar{u}}
, respectively, and conclude that
\xi_{k}=0
, which in turn owing to (
7.30
) (with
s_{k}
in place of
s^{\prime}_{k}
) yields
|s_{k}|\leq\mathcal{O}(1)|\mathbf{u}_{bm}^{+}-\mathbf{u}^{-}_{bm}|
and by plugging this inequality into (
9.49
) we eventually arrive at (
9.7
). This concludes the proof of Lemma
9.2
in the linearly degenerate case.

9.6.2.
Definition

in the linearly degenerate case

We now show that the limit function
\mathbf{v}
in (

1.1
. We recall that if the
k
-th characteristic field is linearly degenerate the Lax admissibility requirement in condition i) is redundant because contact discontinuities are always admissible. To ease the exposition, we focus on case B) in Hypothesis

We consider the sequence
\{\mathbf{\bar{u}}_{m}(t)\}
defined as in (

) and point out that

(9.50)

\boldsymbol{\beta}(\boldsymbol{\phi}(\mathbf{\bar{u}}_{m}(t),\xi^{m}_{\ell(\mathbf{u}^{\varepsilon_{m}}_{b}(t))+1}(t),\dots,\xi^{m}_{k}(t)),\mathbf{u}^{\varepsilon_{m}}_{b}(t))=\mathbf{0}_{N}\quad\text{for a.e. $t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}$ and every $m\in\mathbb{N}$}.

for suitable functions

. We now fix

such that both (

. Up to subsequences
21
21

21

We explicitly point out that, in general, the particular subsequence such that we have convergence

, so we are not stating that the whole function

converges at almost every point.
, we have

for some limit value

. Owing to (

or

\lambda_{k}(\grave{\mathbf{u}}(t))=\lambda_{k}(\bar{\mathbf{u}}(t))=0\;\text{and}\;\grave{\mathbf{u}}(t)=\mathbf{t}_{k}(\mathbf{\bar{u}}(t),s_{k}(t))\;\text{for some $s_{k}(t)\in[-\delta,\delta]$}.

We now turn our attention to (

Case 1:

\boldsymbol{\beta}(\boldsymbol{\phi}(\mathbf{\grave{u}}(t),\xi_{\ell(\mathbf{u}_{b}(t))+1}(t),\dots,\xi_{k}(t)),\mathbf{u}_{b}(t))=\mathbf{0}_{N}.

for suitable values

We separately consider the following cases.

•

\lambda_{k}(\mathbf{\bar{u}}(t)))>0
. First, we point out that

(9.57)

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{\phi}&(\mathbf{\bar{u}}(t),\xi_{\ell(\mathbf{u}_{b}(t))+1}(t),\dots,\xi_{k}(t))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:giusto}}}{{=}}\boldsymbol{\phi}(\mathbf{\bar{u}}(t),\xi_{\ell(\mathbf{u}_{b}(t))+1}(t),\dots,\xi_{k-1}(t),0)\\

By the definition (
4.40
) there is a solution of (

Case 1b:

(9.60)

,
\mathbf{B}_{22}
,
\mathbf{G}_{1}
,
\mathbf{A}_{22}
,
\mathbf{G}_{2}
are the same as in Hypothesis
1
. In what follows, we will systemtically use the notation

\mathbf{u}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{u}_{1}\\
\mathbf{u}_{2}\\
\end{array}\right)\quad\mathbf{u}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{h},\;\mathbf{u}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{N-h}.

In the proof of
[
9
, Lemma 6.1]
one applies the Slaving Manifold Lemma (see
[
9
, §11]
) and constructs the manifold
\boldsymbol{\phi}
in such a way that, under (
9.59
), there is a solution
(\mathbf{u}_{1}(y),\mathbf{u}_{2}(y),\mathbf{z}_{2}(y))
of (
9.60
) such that
(\mathbf{u}_{1}(0),\mathbf{u}_{2}(0))=\mathbf{u}_{b}
, and

(9.61)

\lim_{y\to+\infty}|(\mathbf{u}_{1}(y),\mathbf{u}_{2}(y))-(\mathbf{w}_{1}(y),\mathbf{w}_{2}(y))|=0,

where
(\mathbf{w}_{1}(y),\mathbf{w}_{2}(y))
denote the first
N
components of a suitable orbit of (
9.60
), lying on the manifold
\mathcal{M}^{0}
defined in
[
9
, §3]

and satisfying

(\mathbf{w}_{1}(0),\mathbf{w}_{2}(0))=\boldsymbol{\psi}_{sl}(\mathbf{\grave{u}}(t),\xi_{h}(t),\dots,\xi_{k}(t))
, where the function
\boldsymbol{\psi}_{sl}
is defined in
[
9
, Theorem 5.2]
.

•

We now recall that, owing to
[
9
, (2.12)]
, if
\alpha(\mathbf{u}(0))=0
and
(\mathbf{u}(y),\mathbf{z}_{2}(y))
is a solution of (
9.60
) then
\alpha(\mathbf{u}(y))=0
for every
y
. Since
\alpha(\mathbf{u}_{b}(t))=0
by assumption, then by combining the second line of (
9.60
) with (
9.61
) and the identities
(\mathbf{u}_{1}(0),\mathbf{u}_{2}(0))=\mathbf{u}_{b}
and
(\mathbf{w}_{1}(0),\mathbf{w}_{2}(0))=\boldsymbol{\psi}_{sl}(\mathbf{\grave{u}}(t),\xi_{h}(t),\dots,\xi_{k}(t))

we eventually arrive at

(9.62)

\mathbf{p}_{\mathbf{u}_{2}}(\boldsymbol{\psi}_{sl}(\mathbf{\grave{u}}(t),\xi_{h}(t),\dots,\xi_{k}(t)))=\mathbf{p}_{\mathbf{u}_{2}}(\mathbf{u}_{b}(t)),

where
\mathbf{p}_{\mathbf{u}_{2}}(\mathbf{u})
represents the projection of
\mathbf{u}
onto the last
N-h
components.

•

We now use again
[
9
, (2.17)]
and conclude that, since
\alpha(\mathbf{u}_{b}(t))=0
, then (
9.62
) is equivalent to

(9.63)

\boldsymbol{\beta}(\boldsymbol{\psi}_{sl}(\mathbf{\grave{u}}(t),\xi_{h}(t),\dots,\xi_{k}(t)),\mathbf{u}_{b}(t))=\mathbf{0}_{N}.

By using the properties of the function
\boldsymbol{\psi}_{sl}
given in the statement of
[
9
, Theorem 5.2]
we can then argue as in
Case 1
above and conclude the proof of Theorem
9.1
.
